### §7.4.2 — The inherited account, stated as an account

**One consequential episode; deliberately not one lifecycle.** The controlling adjudication (`_04`) and its operative integration (`_02 §15.3`) hold that **Care Resolution ≠ Sourcing Selection ≠ Fulfillment Execution ≠ Financial Settlement**, and that these are *native families with their own committers, terminal states and closure horizons* threaded by one risk-adaptive constitution. The separation is not taxonomic tidiness — it is load-bearing, and the reason is recorded: forcing one lifecycle *"collapses the very distinctions that keep commercial config from bending clinical authority — it IS what the ungoverned ChatGPT loop did"* (`_02 §14.B` V1). **A config closes in a *version*; a care resolution closes in a *clinical commitment*.** Different closure horizons cannot share a lifecycle.

**What actually makes the separation decidable is a directional reopening cascade, and it is the most valuable thing in this cohort.** Per `_05 §5.6` (operative product law) and `_02 §14.G` (preserved derivation), with one sentence of it in `_04`:

| trigger | what reopens | what stays closed |
|---|---|---|
| jurisdiction / policy incompatibility | **Sourcing** reopens, or blocks with an honest disposition; payment not captured or released; the patient sees *"cannot be sourced in your jurisdiction,"* not *"processing"* | **Care** — clinical meaning is unchanged. Accountability opens only if a promise was broken |
| equivalence-preserving stockout | **Sourcing** reopens first and binds an equivalent active offer; patient-preference / consent / jurisdiction are **revalidated, not reopened**; payment adjusts if terms differ | **Care** — the clinical commitment survives *if* the fallback is clinically equivalent |
| meaning-changing substitution | **Care** reopens; the clinician must recommit; Sourcing and Fulfillment **wait on the new authorization**; payment holds | nothing — this is the one branch that legitimately reaches back into clinical authority |
| money returned | nothing clinical | **a refund is not a clinical remedy.** Where the clinical consequence is irreversible the projection must keep it open *after* the money is refunded |

**This asymmetry is the composition *for this cohort* — and R37 demotes it from "the operating account" to one commerce specialization inside a larger inherited account.** *"Fulfillment failure reopens sourcing; meaning change reopens care"* tells a downstream author which authority is disturbed by which failure, and **it is absent from `_02 §15`** (`§7.4.5` `INT-2`), so an agent authoring `D2` from `§15` alone inherits the conclusion without the mechanism. **But four triggers are not the account.** The July Care capture carries **nine closure horizons**, each with its own terminal, dormant, transfer, supersede and reopen semantics — *signal · resolution · encounter · order/fulfillment · obligation · episode (concurrently stateful, possibly clinically related) · relationship · longitudinal-context · outcome/learning* — with the explicit note that *"`care_commitment` lifecycle + 'ownership closes care' are `[OPEN]` — not carried as settled."* **A decision can close while things consequent on it are unfinished; monitoring or an unresolved consequence can outlive an order's terminal state.** And the capture adds a **fourth reopening rule this cascade does not carry**: the obligation-family de-overload separates `care_obligation` (owed) from **`service_work_order`** — non-clinical operational work with an SLA that may *gate* clinical flow (biomed ticket, transport, turnover) — under the rule ***"operational reroute ≠ clinical reopen."*** **So the commerce cascade is a correct specialization, not the general law, and it must be presented as such in Output A.** *(Verified at named lines by this seat; the capture is `REVIEW-DRAFT`, FROZEN, and its forensic corrections are unintegrated — see `§7.4.2`'s stack.)*

**Where the authority boundary sits inside one episode — the economic-integrity firewall.** `_05 §5.7` states it precisely and the precision is the point: *the clinical option view is blind to **operator economic advantage**, NOT blind to **patient affordability or access***. Patient price, coverage, expected out-of-pocket, adherence feasibility, access and lead time **may transparently shape a care decision** in shared decision-making. Operator margin, rebate, commission, owned-counterparty preference and revenue target **may not influence clinical ranking** — they may legitimately enter **downstream at sourcing**, inside the clinical envelope, with disclosed incentive lineage. `_05 §5.7` sites the mechanism at **the seam between Care resolution and Sourcing selection** — the separation `§15` fixed. **The separation SUPPORTS the firewall; it is not the firewall** *(corrected R43: R33's "the lifecycle separation and the safety property are the same object viewed twice" is withdrawn — two separate lifecycles can still be joined by a defective ranking mechanism, so separation is necessary and not sufficient)*. `_06 R4` grades the economic-integrity mechanism `HOLDS_DESIGN_UNBUILT`.

**And "the firewall" is one label over THREE distinct laws, of which this cohort carries a narrow reading — `v4_INS_G1B §9.1`.** That gate recovered **seven labels across the estate** (`payment_care_firewall` · economically-blind engine · care-neutral fee firewall · C3.8 fee-firewall · alpha-firewall/`REV-193` · economic-firewalling · recommendation-integrity firewall) and resolved them into three:

| | law | status in the estate |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **`payment_care_firewall` — `payment state ≠ care state`.** *Financial or coverage state **may explicitly constrain execution feasibility**, but must **never silently** author, erase or misrepresent clinical indication, recommendation, order, consent or historical rationale.* The operative words are **explicitly** and **silently** — so financing legitimately bears on whether execution occurs now, where, which pharmacy fulfils it, what the patient can afford, and whether an elective plan defers | **the name that was settled; the primitive EXISTS at `D6 §8.1`**, `covered-doctrine`, on nine C3.5-F2 evidence rows including the breaker *"an invariant, not a setting"* |
| **2** | **economic-influence separation** — must be a **STRUCTURAL, auditable** property (*the recommendation engine cannot read who or how much pays OMNI*), **not a policy**; generalizes Law 1. **And the influence sources are nine, not one: payer · PBM · sponsor · manufacturer · lender · referral source · operator · provider organisation · OMNI itself.** A payer's cost interest **may** legitimately decide what it funds; it may **not** be presented as clinical appropriateness unless the proper clinical authority independently commits that conclusion | **`REV-185`, asserted as an invariant, explicitly *"not an object"*; enforcement mechanism + audit spec OWED at C4; open since 2026-06-14. The law that was never given a settled name** |
| **3** | **feasibility legibility** — the **patient's** economic reality **may** bound the feasible set but must remain **separately sourced and recorded** | **normative candidate; no name; home not explicit** |

**What this cohort's firewall reading is, and is not.** `_05 §5.7`'s *"operator advantage out, patient economics in"* is **Laws 2 and 3 compressed into one sentence with only the operator as the influence source.** It is useful in its cohort and is **not** ECB's universal description: *a payer's funding preference, an operator's margin incentive and a patient's inability to afford an option are three different inputs*, and the gate warns that *"economically blind,"* over-read, **collapses Laws 2 and 3.** Two limits the source carries: **Law 3's home is unresolved, not proven absent**, and the nine influence sources are **examples of interested principals, not a closed taxonomy.**

**And Law 1's documented primitive is NARROWER than Law 1 as stated — read at `D6 §8` by this seat, R45.** Invariant 1 is *"a payment failure / subscription lapse / dispute NEVER by itself ends/voids a `care_program` or cancels `clinical_visits`. Program continuity is internal + clinical, not a funding webhook."* **That prohibits termination by funding event.** It does not state the broader prohibition Law 1 carries — *never silently author, erase or misrepresent clinical indication, recommendation, order, consent or historical rationale.* **So *"the name was settled and the primitive exists"* is true and incomplete.** **Stated at the scope the read supports (R46): this is a difference between the WORDING of `§8`'s invariant 1 and the wording of Law 1 — it is not an estate-wide enforcement or semantic gap**, because `§1I.0`, `§12`, `§1K.11` and the `DL-17` chain behind these invariants are unread here, and the accepted Care Lane-1 work separately records `draft_for_ratification` as *"current build-facing carrier, not ratified law or code proof."* **What the paragraph does support: the narrower provision is what is documented, the broader law is what is asserted, and nothing read here closes the distance.** Two related invariants are in the same section and bear on `INT-6`: **inv 5** keeps retail and clinical rails distinguishable per-line with inference-to-classify rejected, and **inv 6** is *"no second source of price."* **A third estate location says the same thing as the separation: inv 2, *"purchases + diagnostic orders do NOT auto-create care_commitments; accountability attaches separately."***

**A named defect on this exact territory, from the accepted Care forensic work — `§G2`, read at R45 and absent from this carrier until now.** Against OFC's five non-collapsing authority gates, *"**the Care capture's authority-table cell ('clinician + payer/eligibility') is the defect** (`OC-AUTHZ`)."* **The accepted forensic audit found a live conflation of clinical authority with payer/eligibility inside the Care capture itself** — which is the failure Law 1 exists to prevent, occurring in an estate artifact rather than hypothetically. And `§H2-LANE5` `T90` carries *"silent care-denial prohibited"* as a **durable principle whose detection, proof, remedy and authority mechanism is an unresolved choice** — a second location for Law 1's *silently* clause, naming enforcement as the gap. **`_06 R4` grades the economic-integrity mechanism `HOLDS_DESIGN_UNBUILT`, with all five fixtures answered in bounds and enforcement `B`** — verified verbatim by this seat, including that its *"incumbents structurally can't"* claim is **demoted to a Task-D hypothesis.**

**The disposition, stated once: three laws at three different DOCUMENTED maturities, and none of that is evidence about implementation.** Law 1 documented but narrower than stated, in a draft contract · Law 2 (`REV-185`) an asserted structural invariant with **enforcement mechanism and audit spec OWED at C4, open since 2026-06-14** · Law 3 unnamed with no explicit home. **`_06 R4`'s scoped design/unbuilt finding stands.** *(Lineage: R42 claimed `R4` was refuted because the primitive exists at `D6 §8.1` — wrong twice, since a documented provision in a `draft_for_ratification` contract is not implemented enforcement, and `R4`'s subject is economic-integrity precision while `R5` is payment sequencing. Withdrawn R43; details at `§16`.)* **The guard runs one way only: documentation-versus-implementation holds regardless of ratification status, and does NOT license *the container is a draft, therefore every invariant inside it is provisional.*** → `Q-SEL`, `Q-SEL-2`, `H-NP`, `D6`, `D8`, `PD-5`.

**Three custody meanings, activating only on a real transfer** (`_04`; `_02 §15.5`): **change custody** — who carries an approved change from review into effective operation · **execution custody** — who accepted responsibility to perform the order, shipment, procedure or task · **consequence custody** — who currently owns the unresolved patient, operator, financial or response consequence. *"The accepted-custody law applies only when one of these actually transfers — it does not force every transaction to invent a custody event."*

**How independently governed participation actually appears — and it is a relation, not a member.** `§15.3`'s family list enumerates **only the lifecycles OMNI governs**. The independent participant enters the operative account three ways and no fourth: as the counterparty end of the `offered_to` / `accepted_by` typed relations (`§15.4` Layer 4) · as `_05 §5.6`'s explicit `counterparty custody offer → custody acceptance/rejection` step · and as `_05 §5.6.1`'s participation ladder. **`_02 §14.B` item 6 and `§14.E` row 1 do name it as a member — *"[EXTERNAL] the vendor's own private workflow — OMNI does NOT own it; observes boundary evidence + custody transitions + proof only"* — and that member did not survive into `§15`'s list** (`§7.4.5` `INT-1`). Whether the external participant *should* be a named member of the composition or is correctly represented as a relation is not settled by this cohort. **It is `D1`, and it is the question ECB exists for.**

**The participation ladder is the most directly ECB-relevant object in the cohort and the carrier under-reflects it.** `_05 §5.6.1` — five modes, *"laws identical at every rung"*: **(1)** native API/EDI/webhook — machine custody events · **(2)** vendor-portal adapter or agent-assisted operation with human confirmation · **(3)** structured secure message / fax / email ingestion — parsed and **attributed**, custody explicit and never inferred · **(4)** operator-entered external report with **explicit degraded provenance** · **(5)** direct counterparty OMNI surface. The invariants at *every* mode: **transport ACK ≠ accepted custody · externally-reported state is attributed (who said so, when, how) · unobserved downstream state stays UNKNOWN · the patient projection may not manufacture closure.** The strategic content is the correction that produced it: **counterparty adoption is not a Day-1 prerequisite** — Mode 5 is the target, not the gate.

**Money is sequenced against admissibility and custody, not against the calendar.** `_05 §5.6.2`: `preflight (offer validity · jurisdiction · consent · fulfillment feasibility) → payment AUTHORIZATION HOLD → counterparty custody acceptance → CAPTURE → settle`, with advance capture an **explicit policy exception** for vendors who require prepayment. The stated principle is *prevention over recovery*: `A6` must not merely *observe* paid-but-undelivered duration better if OMNI could have **prevented** the premature capture. `_06 R5` grades it `HOLDS_DESIGN_UNBUILT_PARTIAL` — prevention is not always available, so honest per-state communication carries the residual.

**Operator configuration is the run's genuinely new object, and its span is why.** `_04` rejected *both* prior positions — Opus's *"custody-free and remedy-free"* (an artifact of truncating the lifecycle at publication) **and** Gemini's *"it is a Platform-Loop specialization"* (which would hand tenant business semantics to OMNI's own release machinery). The landing: **a domain-native, cross-domain governed operating-change lifecycle with release-like controls**, semantic commitment owned by Settings/D6/Federation/D7/RBAC plus operator authorities, Platform permitted to validate/distribute/operate technical effectivity only. Full span (`_05 §5.5`): `vendor discovery → offer evaluation → concept/equivalence mapping → catalog adoption → pricing and discount policy → consent applicability → authority review (with impact assessment on in-flight matters) → publication/effectivity → runtime consumption → monitoring → suspension/supersession → impact assessment (who relied on the superseded version) → rollback/downstream correction`. **Name deliberately deferred to C5 dedup.** The small-practice guard is explicit and honest: trivial changes must pass with *near-zero ceremony*, ceremony scales with risk — and it is marked `ASSUMED → must be proven`.

**The projection owns nothing, and the open dependency is who then acts.** `§15.4` Layer 5: the projection *"owns no truth, owns no obligations, commits nothing, routes nothing, reopens nothing, remedies nothing"*; profile-specific controllers act from it under their own authority; **no universal CNS executive.** `_03` accepted that and wanted CNS named as *the* orchestrator firing the typed seams, because *"a mere read-only projection cannot enforce continuity if it lacks a controller"* — `_04` rejected the universal-executive half and **allocated seven controllers explicitly**: care/cross-domain coordination to CNS *where OMNI workflow semantics require it* (clinical domains retain commit) · sourcing to a sourcing/operational decision controller, *"potentially CNS-coordinated"* · fulfillment to OFC · operator configuration to Settings/D6/Federation/RBAC owners plus a configuration-publication controller · Platform to E&V/Release/Runtime · Accountability to its own controller · settlement to D6/payment-rail reconciliation. *"No loop is a child of CNS."*

**So controllers are named, and `_03`'s `C6` falsifier is still live at a narrower target** — *"a state transitions correctly, but no agent/controller acts on the projection to move the workflow."* **The current account of what is and is not established sits at `PD-8`**, which now carries the Accountability coordinator/ledger split, `response_obligation`'s clock-and-escalation semantics where `breached|overdue` escalate rather than close, C4.5 `T-16`'s *"no single actor owns the COMPOSED future"* with per-element owner/authority/**horizon**, and Demand `§B5`'s ruling that Accountability owns the response **after breach** and never the original obligation. **The residual is the seam: nothing yet establishes that a responsibility falling between two profiles is ADMITTED into any record with an owner and a clock in the first place.** → `D1`, `D4`, `D5`, `Q-PROJ`, `Q-RUNTIME`, `PD-8`, `REV-141`. *(Lineage of this paragraph — a withdrawn "no owner by construction" claim, its two aggravating conditions, and a withdrawn "better question" that OFC had already answered — is at `§16` R36/R37. No universal guarantor is proposed; inventing one would re-create the executive `_04` rejected and breach OFC `§5`'s scope discriminator.)*

### §7.4.6 — Contradictions and unresolved dependencies carried forward

**Preserved disagreements — three, and they must not be smoothed** (`_02 §12`, `§14.K`):
1. **Unified obligation ledger versus projection.** `_01` (blind) makes the Obligation Ledger a **first-class entity** tracking what is owed to patient and to business — **one of its genuinely independent contributions, since `§2.G` did not stipulate it** (`INT-6`). `_03` accepts read-model-only **but requires a named controller to consume it**, and names CNS. `_04`/`§15` accept read-model-only, **reject the universal executive, and allocate seven profile-specific controllers**. **Three positions, one survivor — and the survivor's residual is `controller boundaries`, with `_03`'s `C6` as its live falsifier** (`§7.4.2`). Live for `D1`/`D4`/`Q-PROJ`/`Q-RUNTIME`. **A further lead exists in the live records and no member of this cohort cited it (R34): `D0THES-REV-090` carries a projection doctrine with an explicit event-sourcing two-layer design** — *claim ledger = immutable event log; reconciled entities = materialized aggregates* — with a **binding replayability invariant**, display-projection-not-substrate discipline, and *"projection-cache table admissible only with a clear invalidation contract."* **Corrected R36: it may not even be a fourth POSITION, because it may be ORTHOGONAL.** *"Read-only projection"* does not mean *"view over destructively overwritten records"* — a projection can read **preserved, versioned** domain evidence — **so authoritative ownership and historical-storage mechanism are not necessarily competing architectures.** **And three questions must stay separate: who OWNS an obligation · what historical basis can be RECONSTRUCTED · what CAUSES the next responsible action.** An immutable log answers only the second; **it can preserve an inaccurate assertion perfectly**, and it makes no controller respond. The row is `open`, `architecture_steward_required`, routed to Phase G. **This is a registry row, not the body** — its native scope and applicability must be established at the parent before it is used here (`§13.0` ②), and it is a required entry at `§15.4b`.

**A COMPOSITION HYPOTHESIS — corrected at R38, because R37 overstated it as "substantially one question" and mis-cited one of its three supports.** The observation that survives is narrower and still useful: **the same design move — *derive rather than store* — appears in three places.** July Care `§5` option **(b)** for `care_commitment` is *"projection over resolution+owner+obligations+orders"* · `REV-090` proposes *claim ledger = immutable event log; reconciled entities = materialized aggregates* with a binding replayability invariant · and the July capture describes a derived *"who/what blocks the next action"* view.

**Citation corrected, and the error is the exact hazard this carrier is tracking.** **The `D5` contract specifies `care_state_view`. It contains NO `care_blocker_state` — verified by direct query: `care_state_view` twice, `care_blocker_state` zero times.** `care_blocker_state` is the **July capture's own shorthand** for a concept it attributes to `D5`'s `care_state_view`, and the accepted ledger separately records that **`D5` currently lands only the `responsible_party` expression**, not a named blocker object. **R37 asserted `care_blocker_state` was "already an existing `D5` projection" — a capture's terminology carried forward as an established contract object without opening the purported owning contract.** *And R37 had it RIGHT at `PD-8` (*"a `D5 care_state_view` projection"*) and wrong here: inconsistent inside one revision, which is the eighth instance of this class.* **The projection displays a responsibility; its existence establishes nothing about who enforces it.**

**And the unification is withdrawn: four distinct questions remain distinct, per the reviewer's separation.** *what constitutes the accountable undertaking, and does it need a distinct record, an interface, or a composition* (Care `§5` / `REV-141`) · *what evidence and changes are preserved, and how states are reconstructed* (`REV-090`) · *how an actor obtains a consistent view of stage, blocker and next responsibility* (`D5` `care_state_view`) · *which authorized actor responds, under what conditions, and what happens when none does* (`C6` / `PD-8`). **The counterexample that defeats equivalence: preserve every event perfectly, reconstruct the correct commitment state, and display the correct responsible party — and still have no controller respond when the deadline passes. Storage and projection can both work while `C6` fails.** **Also not mutually exclusive:** an addressable commitment could be reconstructed from preserved events, and a read-only projection could expose responsibility while relying on other records and controllers for every authoritative transition.

**A RELEVANT OPERATING-PROFILE ALTERNATIVE, with its own unresolved ownership and explicit conditions — `v4_INS_G1B` `§4.4`, `F5`, `§13.3`.** *(R42 called this "a THIRD ARCHITECTURAL KIND" that the Layer-5 prohibition "does not reach," and claimed this carrier had no slot for composition. **All three overclaims are withdrawn at R43** — see the three limits below.)* The contribution is real: the financing gate opens **a second axis** the truth-ownership decompositions do not address — *they all answer "who owns the truth?"; **none answers "what must OMNI assemble?"***

> **A first-class mixed-financing operating profile is required.** It composes coverage and benefit positions · commitments · network and contractual positions · financing arrangements · patient responsibility · estimates · authorization · claims · settlement · correction · care impact · communication · redress · exception custody. ***It owns none of the underlying source truths.*** It nevertheless has its **own assessment lifecycle, views, work ownership, clocks, exception states, communication contracts, proof and measurable outcomes** — *"exactly as C4.3's correction-impact assessment does."*

**Three distinctions it forbids collapsing: `no domain ≠ no product` · `no truth owner ≠ no assessment/work lifecycle` · `no universal position lifecycle ≠ no coherent end-to-end operating assembly`.** The failure mode it exists to prevent is the one this carrier was drifting toward: *"reading 'Insurance is not a domain' as 'Insurance does not deserve a first-class operating assembly.' **That would leave OMNI with correct physics and no product.**"*

**Three limits the source carries, which R42 omitted and which change what this can be used for.** **① Applying it to ECB carries an unresolved dependency of its own.** `F5` requires a derived assessment to have ownership of computation, rule version, source selection, validity, invalidation, recomputation, audience policy, retention, issuance and proof, and its verdict is **assessment-lifecycle ownership UNRESOLVED, with no new source-truth owner** — routed to a future gate as `A-Q12`. **`A-Q12` and `PD-8` OVERLAP; they are not the same question** *(R43 asserted an identity and called the alternative "circular" — both withdrawn at R44 as overcorrection)*. `A-Q12` asks who owns a derived assessment's computation and lifecycle; `PD-8` asks whether the distributed mechanisms actually discharge an unresolved responsibility. **They can come apart: a financing assessment can have an assigned maintainer, correct versioning and timely recomputation while the responsible participant still fails to act on an unresolved external request.** So this alternative **does not by itself resolve `PD-8`** — and **an alternative with unresolved details is not inadmissible or circular; it becomes unsound only if those details are assumed resolved in order to claim the requirement is discharged.** **② It is conditional on an anti-shadow test of TEN positively-demonstrable criteria, and two of them are ECB's own questions — verified at the source by this seat, R45.** `§13.3`(B) routes the profile to `INS-G2-OPERATING-COMPOSITION-AND-SUFFICIENCY`, whose first required output is **a noncanonical composition/read contract over native truths** and whose second is **`A-Q12`, derived-assessment ownership.** Its item 11 then requires Gate 2 to **positively demonstrate** that the profile: owns no source position · has no universal commit authority · cannot write back into payer, Care, pharmacy or `D6` truth · versions and invalidates every derived assessment · preserves each source position independently · **uses CNS orchestration rather than minting a universal Insurance-case god-object** · uses Accountability **only after the duty threshold** · cannot silently become a clinical gate · cannot rank options using an interested principal's economics as clinical merit · **cannot turn longitudinal visibility into authority.** **And the consequence is stated: *"if the profile cannot satisfy all ten, Axis 2 is wrong"* and the answer reverts.** **Two of the ten match ECB positions stated elsewhere in this carrier:** *visibility ≠ authority* matches `§6.2`(c)'s *"holding the record is not holding the authority,"* and *CNS orchestration rather than a god-object* matches `_04`'s controller allocation. *(R45 called these "arrived at independently." **Withdrawn — there is no basis for that: they may share prior doctrine or a common derivation, and the claim is unnecessary to their usefulness.** Matching formulations across arcs are evidence of a shared position, never of independent corroboration — `H-CIRC`.)* **`§10.B.10` adds the sharpest condition, which this carrier did not have: *"the operating profile FAILS if it preserves formal authority while allowing unbounded administrative burden to become hidden de facto control."*** **That is `H-BURDEN`'s HIDDEN MANUAL RESCUE named as a failure condition for exactly this kind of assembly, in the estate, before ECB raised it** — and it belongs in `§12.2`'s measurement design as an inherited criterion rather than an ECB invention. **All ten conditions travel with the proposal.** **③ "Object / projection / assembly" is not a clean taxonomy, so no category exemption follows.** An assembly can contain records, projections, controllers and interfaces; its parts still need scoped ownership, and **naming the whole an assembly cannot exempt a component from the rule against a projection silently acquiring control or source-truth authority.** *(Also withdrawn: that this carrier's choice space was binary. `§12.7` `D2` already carries a bounded shared episode/process agreement over local lifecycles and different controller or dependency-evaluation allocations — Insurance supplies substance for that comparison, not a missing slot.)*

**And the opposing hazard is inherited from the same document:** `§4.3` records as live the risk that *"an analytics projection treated as truth"* is exactly what a longitudinal financing-context projection becomes if it acquires a write-back path or becomes a gate. **The guard against a god-object must not become a guard against an operating model — and the reverse. ECB must compare this arrangement against the inherited controllers, projections and `D2`'s other composition alternatives; it settles nothing on its own.**

**So the hypothesis is restated: these mechanisms may compose without another truth-owning object — and *without a truth owner* does not mean *without an assembly*.** **Source coverage remains partial:** `INS_G1B §4.3`/`§4.4`/`§9.1` read at R42 by this seat · the named Care passages, accepted-ledger rows and `D5` naming checks at the depths in `§13.2a` · `REV-090`'s native scope unestablished · the Care capture's corrections unintegrated. → `D1`, `D2`, `Q-PROJ`, `PD-8`. **It belongs to the next unit as a named hypothesis, and it is one reason that unit must not enter through an action-brief lens alone.**
2. **`counterparty_offer`'s home** — Federation (vendor topology) versus Settings (catalog variant) versus a new bounded family. `CONFLICT_REQUIRES_DECISION`, routed to C5, undecided.
3. **Whether *authorize-concept / source-offer* needs a new object or is a Settings/D6 profile.** Undecided — and it is the *"is this overbuilt"* question `F-DIRECT-PROCUREMENT` exists to answer.

**Two structural cautions inherited with the account.** `_00 §3.4` found **no cross-contract ownership conflict** for the cluster — *"the gaps are thinness/absence + build, not contradiction"* — with one watch item, `M5` COGS→margin sitting at a D6/BIZOPS seam neither fully claims. **Absence of contradiction is not evidence of sufficiency**, and `_00 §3.5` says so in its own words. Separately, `_02 §14.B` V4's rejection preserved a truth ECB must keep reachable: **no new domain and no new orchestrator** — *"'spread it across contracts' WITHOUT typed profiles/envelope/custody-transitions/reopening-rules/closure-horizons/projections is exactly the hand-waving that leaves the operator quarterbacking the seams."* That is the inherited argument **for** `D2`'s simplification alternative and **against** its laziest form, in one sentence.

### §7.4.7 — Consequences for ECB's own hypotheses and decisions

**`H-CONC` stands against this cohort, and now has an inherited comparator instead of an invented one.** Verified two ways across all eleven files: **zero occurrences of any resource-reservation, contention, expiry or exclusivity semantic.** The account has exactly three commitment-shaped objects — **explicit counterparty custody acceptance** (`custody offer → acceptance/rejection`, never fiat), a **payment authorization hold**, and **offer adoption/activation** (`prospective → evaluated → adopted → active → suspended`). None is a hold on a scarce excludable resource. `_02 §14.D`'s one use of *"hold"* is about an in-flight consequence, not a reservation. **The inherited strategy for scarcity is re-selection, not reservation:** a stockout reopens sourcing and binds an equivalent active offer. **That supplies `H-SPEED` with *an* inherited comparator — not the established strongest one:** `H-SPEED`'s falsifier requires the comparison be **earned under comparable resources, safety requirements and costs**, which no inherited material does. `Q-COMMIT` gains a concrete inherited floor. **And per `§7.4.8` ④, substitutability and allocation stay separate questions** — this is a bounded coverage debt, not an estate-wide absence.

|---|---|
| **`H-CONC`** · `Q-COMMIT`(×3) | **A bounded COVERAGE debt, verified against all 28 fixture bodies — not an estate-wide capability absence** *(narrowed R36)*. Commitment-by-acceptance and a **payment** hold exist; **this cohort contains no contention fixture at all** — `F-STOCK-EQ` is one actor meeting an unavailable resource, and nothing puts **two claimants on one excludable unit**. **So the cohort does not demonstrate safe concurrent allocation, and its stockout/re-selection cases do not discharge a two-claimants/one-resource requirement.** **And substitutability must be assessed SEPARATELY from allocation** *(R35 conflated them; withdrawn)*: a **fungible** item can have **one acceptable unit left**, where two claimants both observe availability and re-selection resolves nothing without an allocator · and a **non-fungible** resource may still have acceptable alternatives — another room, another eligible time, another qualified person — so re-selection is **not categorically unavailable** merely because the resource is an OR minute or a clinician-hour. **Substitution decides which alternatives are acceptable; allocation decides which claimant may rely on which available capacity; they operate together.** The candidate realizations are therefore plural: a resource owner's **existing booking authority** · serialized acceptance · a queue · a reservation · an explicitly negotiated commitment. **`Q-COMMIT-3`** has a partial inherited answer — `_05 §5.6`'s custody offer/acceptance is commitment *without* command. **And the estate-level question is now narrower than `§15.4b` item 4 assumed — `D3` read at R42.** The scheduling contract already carries **`slot_offered` → `slot_held` (per-class TTL) → `appointment_booked`, with live-state revalidation gating the hold→book transition** and atomic capacity/staff/room/resource treatment. **So a local holding mechanism with expiry EXISTS in a contract.** The cohort-scoped absence claim above survives unchanged — `EVRUN-000012` contains none of this — **but ECB must not approach scarcity as though OMNI lacks a hold.** The remaining question is **how local commitments compose, fail, expire or release across independently governed participants**, which is the `§7.4.8` ④ allocation question and not an absence. **`D3` is `draft_for_ratification`; Burden-as-de-facto-control is **redirected authority** — administrative exhaustion can steer care with no rescue team in existence. One is a cost defect, the other an authority defect, and a burden ledger can catch either while missing the other.)* **`§12.2` should carry both, and the second at its inherited strength: it is a Gate-2 acceptance condition in its own arc, not a measurement suggestion.** The native passage separates authority · leverage · influence · burden incidence · default power, and that separation is the useful inheritance. it does not detect an adverse event that the duty-holder missed. `§11.6`'s asymmetric-detection obligation is therefore **undischarged, not unreachable**. **And R37 adds an inherited position that materially sharpens both hypotheses, verified at the July Care capture's own line: a `care_commitment` attaches when an authorized human or organization *BEARS a scoped care responsibility or consequential stance* — NOT only at a physical action.** A committed monitoring stance, a consult opinion, an accepted plan, a follow-up duty, or *a clinically authorized **no-action** decision* each creates accountability **with zero downstream physical or operational action** — *"an authorized action is one possible manifestation, not the threshold."* **For ECB's external boundary this is load-bearing: a participant can bear accountability without acting, so OMNI's inherited *custody-accepted → executes* pattern is one manifestation of standing rather than its definition.** → `H-STAND`, `H-RECORD`, `D8`, `Q-AUTH` |

### §7.4.8 — Where this cohort's account sits inside rules the estate already carries

**What this section is, after R45.** It was six *"implications at 2030/2035 weight"* labelled *consequences, not decisions.* **That label was doing work only evidence can do, and it is withdrawn as a warrant: an explicitly identified conjecture must still disclose its basis and remain a conjecture, and calling prose a "consequence" makes nothing adequately grounded.** Three of the six carried unsupported extrapolation and are **removed** — the scale prediction that the cascade is *"the difference between graceful degradation and a clinician queue that grows with vendor count,"* the ranking of the unenforced firewall as *"the highest-leverage item in the cohort,"* and the comparative claims about what incumbents can or cannot reproduce *(already withdrawn at R38)*. What replaces them is the result the R45 reads actually produced, and it is more useful than the predictions were: **most of what this cohort states as operating law is a SPECIALIZATION of a general rule the estate already carries, and the specializations are where the cohort adds value while the general rules are where its scope limits live.** Each item below names its support.

**① The cohort's operating rules are specializations; three general forms sit above them in the temporal charter** *(C4.5 `T-11`/`T-12`/`T-16`/`T-17`/`T-18`, read at R45; charter accepted, **pass not started**, several items Knox-patched)*.

| this cohort's rule | the general form already in the estate | what the general form adds |
|---|---|---|
| the four-trigger reopening cascade (`_05 §5.6`) | **`T-18`** — *reopening is selective and continuity-preserving: new info reopens only lifecycles materially dependent on the **changed semantics**; surviving commitments/obligations stay visible* | the *governing-basis / material-basis* principle is **inherited, not authored** — R36 credited it to the reviewing seat, R37 re-credited it to the July capture, and it is **also** a named charter item. The cohort supplies four worked triggers; the charter supplies the rule |
| profile-specific controllers with no universal executive (`_04`) | **`T-16`** — *"no single actor owns the COMPOSED future"*; each goal, forecast, plan, commitment, obligation and constraint retains an explicit owner, authority **and horizon** | **horizon** is worth naming, but *(narrowed R46)* it is **not a newly discovered missing dimension** — OFC's admission test already requires a due window or recurrence, and Accountability's `response_obligation` already carries `due_at`/clock and escalation. **The needed work is to distinguish those temporal meanings from each other — due window · recurrence · clock · escalation trigger · supervision horizon · trust horizon — not to add a third noun.** A horizon is not automatically a deadline |
| ACK ≠ custody · UNKNOWN stays unknown · no manufactured closure (`_05 §5.6.1`) | **`T-20` is the near-exact match, and R45 missed it one clause away while claiming the inheritance from `T-17`** *(corrected R46)*: *"External temporal state is honestly incomplete. **Transport ACK ≠ accepted custody; external receipt ≠ use; absence of returned evidence ≠ no impact; external state unknown until evidenced.**"* `T-17` is the adjacent but **different** provision — seven first-class temporal-uncertainty kinds including silence and missing telemetry | **The two are not interchangeable, and R45's "partial rediscovery" claim collapsed them.** `T-20` governs the **semantic status of an external undertaking**; `T-17` governs **temporal uncertainty about evidence**. *A refusal can be known to the second; an acceptance can be established while its effective time is uncertain.* **Both must coexist and neither replaces the other** — so ECB's four-state separation of absent answers is **not** a rediscovery of `T-17`, it is the participation-state axis `T-20` names, and the temporal axis sits orthogonal to it. **And `T-20`'s third clause — *absence of returned evidence ≠ no impact* — is an inherited anchor for `H-IMPACT` and `§3.4`** |

**Consequence for the arc, not a prediction:** where ECB states an operating rule, it should check whether the charter already states the general form — and where it does, ECB's contribution is the specialization and the evidence, not the rule. **Reading the charter is not running C4.5's pass** (`FWREG-015`), and `T-01`–`T-10`/`T-13`–`T-15`/`T-19`–`T-22` plus the Minimum Temporal Envelope remain unread here.

**② *Derive rather than store* appears in three places with one of them carrying the guard — a composition hypothesis, unchanged in strength.** July Care `§5` option (b) *"projection over resolution+owner+obligations+orders"* · `REV-090`'s *claim ledger = immutable event log; reconciled entities = materialized aggregates* with a binding replayability invariant · and **C4.5 `T-11`, *"governed as-of reconstruction OWNS NOTHING"*** — *"the sharp god-object rule"* — pinning purpose · cutoff · audience · authority scope · versions · coverage · insufficiency. **`T-11` is the one that states the constraint rather than the design**, and it is the most useful comparison of the three. *(R45 called it "the form the other two must satisfy." Withdrawn at R46: C4.5's own passport reads `analysis_nonbinding` · propose-only pre-spine · **"Binds NOTHING. Every name/law herein (T-xx, layer names…)"** · `Provisional Temporal Constitution v0 ONLY` with the full pass not started and nothing promoted. **A more general wording does not make a nonbinding charter the governing parent of Care's representation choice or of `REV-090`.** Carry it as a candidate constraint at its actual status, with an applicability argument — which is exactly `§13.0` ⑥'s rule that a source may materially IMPROVE a candidate without acquiring authority to BIND it.)* **The four questions stay distinct** (`§7.4.6`): what constitutes the accountable undertaking · what can be reconstructed · how an actor sees stage/blocker/next-responsibility · which authorized actor responds. **The counterexample still holds: preserve every event, reconstruct the correct state, display the correct responsible party — and still have no controller act when the deadline passes.** `REV-090`'s native scope is unestablished; no 2035 forecast is offered *(R35's was withdrawn at R38)*. → `D1`, `Q-PROJ`, `Q-RUNTIME`, `PD-8`.

**③ Two worked composition patterns, read at R45, that `D2` currently lacks instances of** *(Platform loop capture, `analysis_nonbinding`)*. **`desired_state`:** *each SOURCE owns its intent*; `resolved_desired_state` is *"a derived, lineage-bearing Platform projection"*; the reconciliation engine consumes it; **conflict decisions are governed and persisted separately from the projection.** **`environment`/`deployment_target`:** ***"OPEN — do NOT introduce a 4th plane by fiat"*** — a registry canonical for identity, Release consuming it as a target, Runtime holding operational custody, the operator owning jurisdictional policy. **One object split four ways across existing owners with no new owner minted, and the residue left explicitly open rather than resolved by fiat.** **These are useful CANDIDATE PATTERNS for separating source intent, derived views, operational custody and unresolved ownership** *(narrowed R46: R45 said they "instantiate" `D2`'s bounded-shared-agreement alternative. **A responsibility table does not demonstrate an agreed cross-participant process, still less its execution** — and Platform's planes are OMNI-internal, not independently governed participants, which is `D2`'s actual subject.)* **The discipline is the transferable part: an unresolved composition is named OPEN, not closed by minting a plane.** Add Demand `§B5`'s diagnostic for the other direction: **composition failure has six candidate causes** — a missing seam · incorrect visibility rules · missing commercial-obligation semantics · a delegation gap · a dispute/remedy gap · **or** a genuinely distinct lifecycle — *"composition failure does not auto-prove a dedicated lifecycle."* → `D2`, `D1`, `PD-8`.

**④ Scarcity remains a bounded coverage debt, now qualified in three places rather than one.** The cohort contains **no contention fixture** and its stockout cases do not discharge a two-claimants/one-resource requirement — that stands, verified against all 28 fixture bodies. **And it is not an estate-level absence:** `D3` already carries `slot_offered` → `slot_held` (per-class TTL) → `appointment_booked` with live-state revalidation gating the hold, and **C4.5 `T-12` names `reservation` as a non-collapsible future semantic role** alongside plan, commitment, obligation and order. **Substitution decides which alternatives are acceptable; allocation decides which claimant may rely on which capacity; they operate together** — so the live alternatives are plural: a resource owner's existing booking authority · serialized acceptance · a queue · a reservation · a negotiated commitment. **The option most consistent with Layer 3's no-central-table law is to REFERENCE or COORDINATE an existing authoritative allocator rather than become one** — an existing scheduler is domain-owned truth reached by a typed relation. Inventory, OFC and facility remain unreconciled; **no reservation architecture is implied or required.** → `H-CONC`, `H-SPEED`, `Q-COMMIT`, `D2`, `D3`.

**⑤ The restraint, which is the one item that was never a prediction.** Items ① and ③ both make OMNI's responsibility look larger, and `§15.7`'s recorded execution risk cuts the other way: *"a hard responsibility mistaken for a defensible business."* **Nothing here establishes that OMNI should own any of it.** `_07 §7.13`'s honest verdict is that the defensible centre is *a candidate, not a proven, advantage*; `_06 §6.5` defers copyability and the moat to the incumbent-composition contest; and `§12.6` keeps *the incumbent bundle supplies this span with less total burden* a legitimate result. **Both directions stay reachable.**

### §7.4.9 — The operating account as it now stands · consolidated 2026-09-19 *(and the synthesis checkpoint `§13.0` ③ requires)*

**Why this replaces a checkpoint.** Through R46 this section recorded *what the unit had not retained.* **A catalogue of what remains unread is not an operating account**, and `§12.9.6` makes `§7.4` the home of the current one. What follows states, for the arrangements actually examined: **what the inherited arrangement does · which authority decides what · what may remain concurrently open · which candidate addresses which part · and the exact relationships still missing.** Every element carries its source and its authority. **Nothing here is accepted architecture** (`§12.4`), and **the operative section for any element is its own home, not this summary.**

#### ① What the inherited arrangement does — and it is ONE ratified spine, which this arc spent forty-six revisions circling

**`doctrine/omni_enterprise_posture_2026-06-03.md` is `ratified` (Nick + Knox, 2026-06-03) and `governance_binding`, and it carries Governed Capability Exchange as the operational capstone under `D0THES-DEC-036`.** `§7` cited that decision ID in one clause and **no ECB seat opened the capstone until R47.** Its spine, verbatim:

> `actor / represented-principal → capability contract → Identity → Federation boundary → RBAC capability → delegated authority → context packet → consent/grant → CNS orchestration → owning-domain commit → audit/proof → returned artifact/status` — classified as **evidence | observation | proposed-meaning | `externally_committed_truth`** *(committed in the source system, **NOT OMNI-committed**)* — ***"BEFORE it counts."***

**And the answer to whether internal and external exchange are the same shape is ratified, not open:** *"**Two faces, one spine** — internal (`D7`→Observation→Clinical Memory; `D5`→`D6`→BIZOPS; Settings→`D3`) and external (OMNI ↔ lab / Rx / insurance / payroll / supplier / marketplace / external EMR / portal / scheduling agent / partner operator). **External adds trust-transfer + metadata-custody risk** (`D0THES-GRD-030`); **the governance spine is identical.**"* Two keeper lines bound what that means. **`GRD-034`: *"OMNI is NOT measured by number of integrations; it is measured by whether every exchange preserves identity, authority, consent, context, ownership, commit, and proof. Bounded, designed surface — expanded by doctrine pass, never connect-everything."*** **`GRD-033`: *"rail-agnostic, protocol-agile, vendor-replaceable, and semantics-stable. MCP / A2A / API / FHIR / EDI / portals / browser-and-voice agents / future protocols are replaceable adapters. OMNI owns the capability contract, authority envelope, context packet, trust-transfer record, domain-commit boundary, and audit/proof."*** **So the connector is deliberately the commodity layer, and the six things OMNI owns are the product.** Plus the anti-corruption rule: *"external systems are rails/processors/counterparties, **never source-of-truth owners** — PayChex executes payroll; BIZOPS still owns labor truth"* — refined at `C4.6` into the precise form ECB should use: **an external system MAY be authoritative for facts committed within its own sovereign scope while owning none of OMNI's resulting canonical state**, with any OMNI consequence resolved through the owning domain.

**Where this leaves the arc's crudest and best question — an async intake questionnaire versus a robot cutting tissue.** **`P35` is *"External Capability / Signal-Command Boundary"*, and it was deliberately renamed away from *"Device-Link / Telemetry-Ownership"* as *"too device-flavored"* precisely so it would span *"ANY external system OMNI connects to (watch, vent, HR monitor, **Tesla surgical robot**, lab analyzer, IV pump, phone/PBX, cafeteria system, camera…)."*** **So the estate's position is that they share the boundary object and the governance spine — and that is where the comparison STARTS.** `§6.3`'s own caveat governs the rest and must not be skipped: ***"an adequate asynchronous review arrangement is not automatically an adequate real-time supervisory arrangement."*** **Determining which constraints demand materially different control, evidence and responsibility arrangements is the work `D1`/`D4` exist to do — *"same physics under different constraint"* frames it and settles nothing.**

#### ② Which authority decides what

**Per the spine, authority is distributed across steps rather than held by a layer**, and the inherited allocations are: **Identity** resolves who this is, with identity-match confidence separate from authorization to use or share (`L1-CP1`) · **Federation** admits across an operator boundary, and **admission does not replace the owning domain's authority** · **RBAC** decides action- and ownership-dimension-specific capability under default-deny, rechecking at **emission and execution**, and its own non-collapse example is the discipline: *an actor can hold a clinical capability and a valid licence and still lack the current **competency** for the act* · **`D7`** holds the durable consent artifact, with artifact custody separate from permitted use · **delegated authority** is a distinct primitive from technical capability, verified distinct from both `capability_envelope` and `capability_contract` · **CNS orchestrates where OMNI workflow semantics require it, and clinical domains retain commit** — *"no loop is a child of CNS"* · **the owning domain commits.** `_04` allocates seven profile-specific controllers across that spine. **Three firewall laws sit across it** (`§7.4.2`), of which Law 1's documented primitive is narrower than Law 1 as stated and Law 2's enforcement is owed. **And the returned-status classification is itself an authority act:** evidence, observation, proposed-meaning and `externally_committed_truth` are four different things and only the fourth is a commitment — *in someone else's system.*

#### ③ What may remain concurrently open — the part the account previously got wrong

**Responsibilities coexist; they do not queue.** Accountability admits *"near-misses, unresolved breakdowns, or **unmet obligations**"* with the demarcation *"ordinary forward-flow work stays in its own loop/domain"* — so **an original performance duty and an investigation-or-communication duty run at the same time**, a credible allegation **auto-admits provisionally** before any breach finding, and `breached|overdue` **escalate rather than close** under *"silence is not closure."* The July capture's **nine closure horizons** say the same thing from the lifecycle side: *a decision can close while things consequent on it are unfinished.* C4.5 `T-16` states the governing form — ***"no single actor owns the COMPOSED future"***, with each goal, plan, commitment, obligation and constraint retaining its own owner, authority and horizon. **And Agent Runtime supplies the operational version for a live act: continued-use authorization can be invalidated while the run's receipt of what it saw stays immutable — historical fidelity and current admissibility diverge without contradiction.**

#### ④ Which candidate addresses which part

**For *what constitutes the accountable undertaking*:** July Care `§5`'s four live options — distinct object · projection over resolution+owner+obligations+orders · per-domain interface · **retire** — with the accepted forensic finding framing it **neutrally across realization options and *"do NOT prejudge."*** **For *what can be reconstructed*:** `REV-090`'s event-log-plus-aggregates, and C4.5 `T-11`'s *"governed as-of reconstruction **owns nothing**"* as the constraint any such design must satisfy. **For *how an actor sees stage, blocker and next responsibility*:** `D5`'s `care_state_view`, which **displays** a responsible party and establishes nothing about who enforces. **For *who responds*:** Accountability's coordinator/ledger split, and `INS_G1B §4.4`'s operating profile **as an alternative to compare, carrying its ten anti-shadow criteria and its own unresolved `A-Q12`.** **For *composition without a new owner*:** Platform's `desired_state` and `environment` rows as candidate patterns, with the discipline *"`OPEN` — do NOT introduce a 4th plane by fiat."* **For *how an independent participant enters*:** `_05 §5.6.1`'s five-mode ladder with *"laws identical at every rung"*, and Demand `§B5`'s composition of existing primitives at the edge. **Four questions stay distinct and no candidate answers more than its own** (`§7.4.6`).

#### ⑤ The exact relationships still missing — specific, not a reading list

| missing relationship | status, and where it is recorded |
|---|---|
| **Command-authority mode across the external boundary** — the one dimension on which a pharmacy and a surgical robot genuinely diverge | **`P35` spans that whole range and has NO owner and no positive enforcement carrier at contract depth; `REV-188` live open.** `D4`, `Q-P35`, `§6`'s trauma case |
| **Represented-principal authority** — the FIRST element of the ratified spine | **No first-class surrogate/guardian/represented-principal authority carrier; artifact-level signing only** (`SC-L1-07`). **A gap at step 1 of the spine**, and `§2.2`'s *who authorizes THIS act* |
| **Supervision of a continuously unfolding act** | Point-of-action authorization and continued-use invalidation are inherited; **monitoring, inhibition, interruption and bounded safe continuation are not established anywhere read** (`H-DUR` ③) |
| **`care_team_graph` derivation** | Identity→CNS→Identity with nobody defining it (`SC-L1-09`) — an accepted, dated ownership gap, **not a demonstrated `C6` failure** |
| **Economic-influence separation enforcement** | Law 2 (`REV-185`) is an asserted structural invariant with **enforcement mechanism and audit spec OWED at C4**, open since 2026-06-14; `T90`'s *silent care-denial prohibited* names the same missing choice |
| **The operative owner of an outstanding obligation at a seam neither profile claims** | **UNRESOLVED** in Demand's own territory; Accountability owns the response, *"never the original deliverable/obligation"* |
| **`degraded_safety_state`** | No named carrier after two accepted checkpoints; degraded-mode authority open under `REV-188`. `Q-DEGRADE` |
| **Whether the composed safety floor is deterministically enforceable** | `F-FLOOR` `UNKNOWN_REQUIRES_DEDUP` → C3.9/C5 seam check |

#### ⑥ Portability, stated from the ratified posture rather than re-derived

**Semantics-stable with replaceable rails is the ratified position** (`GRD-033`), so **portability cannot degrade by participant type — the spine does not branch on what the counterparty is; it branches on trust-transfer and metadata-custody risk.** `C4.6` makes the content concrete: **origin context is ORTHOGONAL to the exchange mechanics** — medspa visit · asynchronous visit · ordinary ambulatory · emergency · inpatient · discharge/transition — and **a medication list or reconciliation record is *not itself* an executable prescription**, while a discharge transition must not lose prescription lineage or continuation responsibility. **So portability is transferring meaning and basis adequate for the intended use, and it confers nothing else: receiving the material does not carry the source's authority, renew an expired permission, or establish the receiver's acceptance.** Runtime supplies the operating counterpart — **preserve what a run saw while invalidating its permission to keep using it.** *(This is a substantive connection among Reservoirs, Time, Identity/RBAC, GCE and portability; it is stated here because the retained account depends on it, and it is **not** declared a completed answer for OMNI.)*

#### ⑦ Simulation — admitted at its actual status, because it bears on how this arc tests anything

**Two different jobs, and collapsing them would be the error.** **(a) Testing the arrangement:** explore failure paths and competing arrangements before exposing real participants. An uncalibrated model can reveal a useful counterexample; it establishes **no** failure probability, safety property or operating economics. **(b) Something OMNI itself may use while operating:** a simulated schedule or anticipated consequence may inform a decision, with its assumptions, scope, evidence quality and authorization consequences kept distinguishable from observed state and accepted commitments. **Neither creates an OMNI Simulation domain.** **Inherited status, at its actual level:** `EVSRC-2026-000242`'s proposed Simulation Lab is **watch-stage material, not an accepted module**, and it explicitly refuses synthetic behaviour as patient consent, clinical truth or outcome proof. **The usable distinction is FAI `§E15.4.1`'s three evidence kinds — prospective assessment (what an issuer thinks may happen) · simulated consequence (what an execution produced under declared inputs, fidelity and coverage) · observed consequence (what execution or observation evidence supports at a declared time)** — plus its separation of a real bounded canary from a no-primary-effect shadow path. **One inherited inference is narrowed rather than adopted:** that an execution producing no commitment-frontier event *"did not touch the world."* **Absence of that record is not proof of absence of effect unless effect mediation and recording coverage are established** — otherwise an unrecorded send, an out-of-band action or a failed capture becomes *"simulation"* by omission, and the same passage already concedes a shadow path may expose real data. **The boundary to carry: a result may be simulated with respect to the target operation while its execution has real data, resource or exposure effects. Record what was simulated, what actually executed, and what the evidence can establish.** *(A review conclusion about a frozen formulation — FAI is not reopened, and `§12.2` consumes this: simulated burden reduction can inform a proposed test; it is never measured ecosystem benefit or evidence that external parties will participate.)*

#### ⑧ Synthesis checkpoint · consolidated 2026-09-19

**Invoked at the unit's stop** (`§13.0` ③, `§12.9.6`). **The six things required before retaining an architecture-changing finding:** source identity and exact sections — `§13.2a` · authority, maturity and supersession posture — recorded per element above, and the governing fact is that **one element is `ratified`/`governance_binding` (the GCE capstone) while everything else is `draft_for_ratification`, an accepted-within-run result, or a nonbinding capture** · native mechanism without OMNI adaptation — quoted above · existing architecture coverage — **partially assessed for the first time at R47 via the capstone, and still unassessed for the two families at `§13.2a`** · rightful destination — none; nothing is routed by this unit · material uninspected scope — `§13.2a` and `§15.4`.

**No architecture-changing finding is retained, and none is proposed.** **What is retained is an inherited arrangement with one ratified spine, a distributed authority allocation, coexisting responsibilities, four competing candidates for the accountable undertaking, and eight named missing relationships.** **The single most transferable lesson of unit 1, stated for whoever comes next: the estate's answer was usually already ratified or accepted somewhere, and every seat in this arc — including the replacement — lost more time to not opening the capstone than to any reasoning error.**
