## §8 — Hypotheses

`resolved` / `open` / `insufficient_evidence` are all permitted.

| ID | Hypothesis | Falsifier |
|---|---|---|
| **`H-FOR`** | **Benefit, purchase and authority separate per transaction and act (`§2.2`) — coinciding in one party or distributed across several, with different acts carrying different authorizers — so a product can have an identifiable primary user and purchaser <strong>without that party holding universal authority</strong>. <strong>The test does NOT require exactly three different parties</strong>, and payer coverage is not a universal source of clinical authority.** **`C3.8 G4 §2.2`'s layered direction is inherited, not reopened; what is open is its mechanics and the specific collisions** | **POSITIVE CONSTRUCTION TEST — NOT a falsifier** *(relabelled in R17; see the adjudication below)*. Design a coherent arrangement whose primary user is identifiable without losing another party's rights, duty or authority — and name which collisions the layered direction does not resolve. **Succeeding demonstrates the compatibility the hypothesis proposes; it does not falsify it. The constraint has real teeth in the other direction: a candidate that can achieve an identifiable primary user ONLY by granting the purchaser universal authority FAILS.** **A finding that the layered direction already resolves the collisions is a legitimate result** · **Adjudicated disagreement preserved:** the incumbent Knox review held that the exercise as written constructs an example supporting its own proposition; the fresh-Opus readiness review praised the row as symmetric and decidable. **Both are partly right and R16 recorded neither. Resolution: the exercise is retained and valuable; only its evidentiary LABEL was wrong.** No new hypothesis is minted and the operating model is not reopened |
| **`H-ROLE`** *(replaces `H-BROKER`)* | **What OMNI supplies, and what a credible competing arrangement supplies, are not settled by what its components are called** (`§2.3`). A patient-hub-plus-software-plus-contracted-delivery arrangement may or may not preserve the continuity, accountability and recourse OMNI proposes to preserve | compare **whole arrangements** on the same operating span: what each supplies, which participant retains which responsibility, what it costs, and behaviour when a participant refuses, disappears or withholds internals. **A label test is not admissible evidence** — neither "broker" nor "infrastructure" establishes presence or absence of obligation. Feeds Task D (`§12.8`); does not close it |
| **`H-CIRC`** | **Circular corroboration is unhandled through a full round trip** — Care `§9a`'s *multiplicity ≠ independence* rule exists and has never been tested where OMNI's own output returns as apparent third-party confirmation (`§6.3`) | run the export → outside interpretation → return → review cycle. Detect the false upgrade; and separately detect apparent disagreement caused by different dates, units, versions or questions rather than judgement |
| **`H-SPEED`** | **Candidate, not explanation:** for contention over scarce excludable resources, a material class of delay persists because a holdable commitment is unavailable across organizational boundaries. **It competes with — and does not subsume — evidence reuse, earlier acquisition, reduced reconstruction, preparation/parallelism, safe retry and selective reconsideration** (`§3.1`, `§3.2a`) | Test the **incremental value of the holding mechanism for the named operation and resource class** against its strongest simpler alternative, under comparable resources and safety requirements, **counting the mechanism's own reservation, expiry, contention and maintenance costs and its effects on other participants**. Failure rejects or narrows **this mechanism claim** — it does not reject other routes to timely appropriate action or the arc. **The previous falsifier (*"if the wait is dominated by anything other than renegotiated commitments, the thesis is wrong"*) is withdrawn: it tested a broader claim than the hypothesis makes, and coordination need not dominate total waiting for a bounded mechanism to be worth building.** **Note the reach limit: the separation of delay components needs operating data `§12.7` D6 places out of reach here** (`§7.3`, R3 — owed to pilot). *(R5's falsifier — "if frontier preconditions are long-lived" — was poorly formed and is withdrawn: the value could still come from reduced reconstruction, earlier preparation or less rework.)* |
| **`H-CONC`** | **Concurrent commitment is unspecified** — the inherited candidate names `requires` but no authoritative reservation, contention, expiry/revocation or execution-evidence semantics (`§3.1`, CWE-367) | the tray case: two actors, one resource, both checks pass. Name the owning transition, contention behaviour, expiry, evidence and recovery |
| **`H-ARB`** | **What makes joint activity on one patient permissible and controllable is unmodelled.** *(Reframed from R9's "rights over a shared physical variable," which pre-selected authority allocation as the mechanism — `§6.1`, `§16`.)* | the six-robot case, run without assuming the mechanism. Determine which combinations of behaviour are permitted · which assumptions each participant relies on · which changes require coordination · which conditions must hold **throughout** execution · who detects their failure · what the arrangement may then safely do · and the record of who acted on superseded information. Candidate mechanisms — authority allocation, coordinated simultaneous action, sequencing, inhibition, supervision, shared constraint, reconfiguration — compete; **selecting one is a Pass-3 act, not a premise** |
| **`H-BRIEF`** | **The four jobs — context, live observation, execution conditions, authority — are not composable into a bounded permission-to-act without a gap.** *(R6's "does not exist" is withdrawn: `capability_envelope`, `delegated_authority_envelope`, CNS `§9.1` and thesis v3 §B all exist and are unreconciled.)* | attempt the composition from that family. **If it composes, `H-BRIEF` dies and the finding is a reconciliation, not a gap.** If it does not, name the specific missing job |
| **`H-DUR`** | **Conditions that must hold *throughout* execution are unhandled** — initial authorization is treated as settling the act | an act whose conditions change mid-execution; name what is monitored, by whom, with what fallback and stopping capability |
| **`H-IMPACT`** | Impact reach is **bounded and uncertain**, not exact; some consequential relationships are unrecorded, unobservable or discovered afterward (`§3.4`, C4.3 `10.1`) | the miscalibrated-instrument case where outputs were used outside the instrumented system. **A complete reliance graph does not exist to traverse** |
| **`H-SCALE`** | The inherited candidate holds at the individual regime and has **never been tested** at shared-resource or population regime | run all three regimes (`§11.3`). Local correctness cannot establish global resource consistency, population privacy or network stability. **R5's blanket "never tested at wider scope" is softened: the prior adjudication discusses configuration reaching many patients and C4.3 contains cohort and cross-operator cases — what is unestablished is the full ECB scope** |
| **`H-BURDEN`** | The value chain — maintained evidence → less reconstruction → earlier dependency resolution → less rework → more timely appropriate action — **holds for some operations and not others, and the cost of the mechanism itself is unmeasured** | measure per `§12.2`, including **total ecosystem burden** on patients and counterparties, not only OMNI users |
| `H-READY` | No integrated, owned **readiness composition** has been located — Care `§5b` maps readiness onto operational state, Inventory and Runtime health, and `§14` separates work order, obligation and blocker projection, but nothing composes them against a scheduled act | trace the 30-item case through existing owners |
| `H-TRACK` | The physics apply to tracks with **no clinical owner at all** | run Care `§1a`'s routing rule on the readiness case. **The test is whether an applicable consequential responsibility lacks an owner — not whether every cell is populated** |
| `H-CAP` | **Capability formation from patient context** is unhandled — the candidate may not exist until specimens, experimentation and design produce it, and the process changes what is being evaluated | trace a patient-specific design with a material context change mid-manufacture; name what is revisable, what consumed scarce material, what needs a new commitment |
| `H-OPAQUE` | **Reliance on a valid-but-opaque assertion is unmodelled**; `GRD-017` forbids narration as explainability, so a candidate shape is an attested claim carrying provenance, evaluator independence, error characteristics, scope and expiry — without internals | a participant returns a valid answer and will never expose intermediates |
| `H-RECORD` | **Basis: operator-directed inquiry + proposed synthesis — NOT inherited evidence.** **Inherited support exists and must be composed, not rediscovered:** the Observation contract already separates `media_artifact → observation → extracted_assertion` and records *what was measured and how faithfully*, explicitly NOT the durable artifact and NOT clinical adoption; C4.3 already carries fixtures where the record and the actual event disagree (`note+charge 50 / actual DISPUTED`; `50 administered / 40 intended`). **The open question is composition under the expanded arrangement, not the existence of authored evidence.** The claim that survives: **documentation quality is not interchangeable with performance quality**, and a participant's reported state is a selection from its internal state — for machine participants as much as human ones | **Test whether the proposed arrangement distinguishes source fidelity · evidentiary coverage · completeness FOR THE INTENDED USE · uncertainty about what actually occurred · and later interpretation.** Discriminating cases must include **a correctly limited report** (complete for its purpose, silent beyond it), **a material omission**, **a disagreement between report and execution evidence**, and **a legitimate demand for evidence necessary to rely on another party's work.** **Neither *"accept it because it is signed"* nor *"disclose every internal state"* is an adequate universal answer, and a larger feed is not automatically a better basis.** **Completeness is assessed FOR THE INTENDED USE against applicable requirements: material not required in the operative note may nevertheless be required in another record, disclosure, investigation or response, and author preference does not establish that it may be omitted everywhere** (`D8`) |
| `H-STAND` | **Basis: operator-directed inquiry + proposed synthesis — NOT inherited evidence. R19's "unmodelled" absence claim is WITHDRAWN, verified false.** Care `§9a` already names the heterogeneous topology — *"patient ± private AI · provider ± preferred external AI · OMNI-native assistant · specialist AI · pharmacy AI"* — with participant admission, context admission, authority to commit, represented principal, sponsor/incentive, model version, uncertainty, exposure, **disposition including `unseen`**, influence on later action, and independence-versus-shared-source. Accountability `§§4`–`5a` already separates **reporter from detector**, handles external and weak-identity reporters, and holds that a concern may oblige a response its originator has no authority to dictate. **What is open is composition under uneven detection and real-time physical constraint, not whether participation was ever modelled** | **Test how participation permission · accepted monitoring responsibility · concern submission · response obligation · and intervention authority compose when detection is uneven.** Required cases: **a credible concern from a party with no command authority** · **an obligated monitor that misses or cannot observe the event** · **a false alarm** · **an urgent concern nobody acknowledges.** **Duties and response assumptions are declared BEFORE evaluation, never invented after the result.** **Do NOT infer "owes nothing" from the absence of a provider or OMNI contract** — an undertaking to the patient, or a duty arising from role, law or circumstance, may apply, and actual applicability needs qualified review. **A finding that a given participant owes nothing is legitimate; assuming it is not** |
| `H-POSTURE` | **The general absence claim is WITHDRAWN — verified false in two documents.** `.cursor/plans/omni_thesis_v3_2026-06-03.md` **`§B.5 Autonomy, HITL, evals, lineage`** (L126) states *"autonomy graduation earned by evals/traces"* (L127). Separately `.cursor/plans/omni_thesis_v3_integrated_2026-06-05.md` L3028 states *"Agentic autonomy is not binary… `autonomy_level` A0–X governed by HITL/HOTL/HOOTL gates per capability + risk tier… **Higher autonomy is earned per capability through evals + proof, never assumed**,"* and L3032 that *"evals/runtime-proof gate registry admission and autonomy."* **The live hypothesis is scope:** L2973 confines that mechanism to *"what a model or tool may do"* and says it explicitly does **not** own *"what a non-human actor is authorized to do for a principal."* So nothing establishes an earned-posture pathway for an independently operated device, producer, laboratory or organization. *(Source status: `draft_authoring`, not the ratified replacement — `§13.2`.)* | determine whether the inherited mechanism extends to non-AI independently governed participants, and if so what participant identity, configuration, evidence, evaluator independence, authority boundary and re-evaluation circumstances it requires. **Performance evidence never independently confers professional authority or another organization's delegation** |
| `H-NP` | **Operator non-privilege is untested in both directions** | **affiliation test** — vary affiliation only, no privilege may appear. **Substantive-difference test** — vary delegation, credentialing, custody, jurisdiction, network placement or contractual responsibility; the difference must be **represented, not erased** |
| `H-SURV` | An episode identifier **is not a successor undertaking**; obligations may not survive operator exit, producer insolvency or consent expiry | name who retains or accepts the duty, how refusal is represented, what happens when no successor exists |
| `H-FRAME` | The care↔biology↔evidence↔research↔outcomes frame is not adequately reconciled or routed | attempt the composition from `§15` + `REV-189`/`REV-190` + GCE + P35 + Care `§1a`/`§9a`. **No claim about history or about why prior arcs omitted it** |

---

## §9 — Frontier pressures *(not reducible to one axis)*

R5 reduced the frontier to shortened precondition lifetimes. **Too narrow — a stable fact can be expensive to obtain, reconcile or apply, and a fast-changing fact can be irrelevant to the next act.** The pressures to preserve together: **generated interventions** (the capability may not exist until context, specimens, experimentation and design produce it) · **adaptive execution** (conditions change during an action; admission and initial authorization settle nothing ongoing) · **coupled participants** (independently acceptable actions interacting through physiology, resources, deadlines or objectives) · **multiple scales** (a local observation, batch, model release or policy change affecting many people and organizations) · **independent authority and incomplete visibility** (participants refuse terms, withhold intermediates, change posture, disappear) · **long-lived consequences** (exposure, monitoring, correction and responsibility surviving the original episode and its participants) · **and shortened validity where it genuinely occurs.** These are proof obligations, not predictions that every capability arrives by a date.

---

## §10 — Question set *(stable IDs from R6; R5 map at `§15.3`)*

**Meaning and identity** — `Q-ENT` what kinds of things exist and what the relations mean: person · organization · role · device · model · product definition · offer · lot · physical unit · specimen · aliquot · dataset · derived model · design · dose · observation · plan · reservation · exposure — **not freely substitutable** · `Q-ENT-2` the same moisturizer: catalog definition, vendor offer, physical unit, lot, purchase, aftercare instruction and recorded use are **distinct**; avoiding duplication ≠ one master object · `Q-ENT-3` **material derivation and information derivation are not successive statuses of one patient record**

**State, commitment, dependency, authority, projection, controller, runtime** — `Q-LIFE` valid creation/correction/transition/closure per authoritative record (a payment, order, result, permission and treatment relationship do **not** share one lifecycle because each has a status field) · `Q-PROTO` what a request, offer, acceptance, refusal, delegation, acknowledgment or handoff **means between parties**, and what may be inferred from silence, duplication, timeout, rejection or partial fulfilment · `Q-DEP` **what each typed relation does on change** — update a view · invalidate a candidate · block an unstarted act · demand reassessment of a completed act · create a notification duty · leave the act but alter follow-up. **A graph link alone does none of these**, and `supersedes`/`accepted_by`/`remediates` are not preconditions · `Q-CAUSE` physical and scientific effects need a causal model — **an ontology recording that two controllers influence one variable does not prove the interaction safe** · `Q-AUTH` who may assert, adopt, accept a duty, release a resource, execute, revise or grant use — data ownership ≠ corporate ownership ≠ custody ≠ execution ≠ professional accountability · `Q-PROJ` projection vs controller remit vs owner; **a derived artifact may still have identity, version and a governed lifecycle without owning authoritative state** · `Q-RUNTIME` where state lives, which component checks authority, what happens on delay or failure, which decisions need **current local** facts

**Commitment and concurrency** *(new — `H-CONC`)* — `Q-COMMIT` what makes a commitment **holdable**: authoritative reservation, exclusivity, expiry, revocation, contention behaviour, evidence, recovery · `Q-COMMIT-2` which preconditions **can** be converted into commitments and which can only ever be evaluated · `Q-COMMIT-3` cross-organization commitment where no party has delegated command

**The action brief** *(new — `H-BRIEF`)* — `Q-BRIEF` what a specific actor needs for a specific authorized act: minimum sufficient facts and their time basis · **what must remain true during execution** · authority and scope · held commitments · mid-act fallback · expiry and early revocation · `Q-BRIEF-2` is it a profile of CNS `§9.1`'s context packet or a distinct object · `Q-BOUNDARY-2` is it the same object as GCE's boundary exchange

**Joint activity on one patient** *(`H-ARB`)* — `Q-ARB` **what makes joint activity permissible and controllable**, without presupposing the mechanism: which combinations of behaviour are permitted · which assumptions each participant relies on · which changes require coordination · which conditions must hold **throughout** execution · who detects their failure and what may then safely happen · `Q-ARB-2` composed control responsibility, interaction constraints, timing, conflicting objectives, and who resolves a conflict · `Q-ARB-3` the record of what each participant was told, when, and whether it acted on superseded information · `Q-ARB-4` what OMNI does when a participant **refuses** the coordination protocol (`§6.2`) · `Q-ARB-5` **which parts of this require clinical and control-engineering validation that this arc cannot supply**

**Posture and command** — `Q-P35` **`REV-188`** ownership (distribute · one owner · or explicitly re-defer with a reason) · `Q-P35-2` binding grain · `Q-P35-3` are supervision, delivery mode, emergency authority, execution delegation and autonomy degree separate dimensions, reconciled against Care `§5b.1`'s existing factoring · `Q-RECEIPT` what positively constitutes an execution receipt · `Q-DL18` the `Q-DL18-4` delegated-authority-grain dependency — **described, never chosen** · `Q-FED` Federation's missing non-human/agent modelling (**default: route**) · `Q-TIME` control-timescale separation · `Q-DEGRADE` validated local behaviour on connectivity loss — **no universal stop-everything or continue-everything fallback**

**Readiness** — `Q-READY` who owns assembling resources, items, instruments, implants, consumables, suppliers, facility capacity, sterile-processing turnaround and on-site personnel · `Q-READY-2` how a supplier or consignment holder learns what is needed, when, on whose authority · `Q-READY-3` what readiness state must exist before execution authorization, and who may refuse on readiness grounds · `Q-READY-4` **readiness is time-sensitive evidence and held commitments over actual resources — never a stored green flag** · `Q-READY-5` is `care_coordination_owner` the owner of readiness or a distinct role

**Discovery, privacy, population** — `Q-DISC` are aggregate opportunity · cohort feasibility · anonymous eligibility · notification · re-contact · clinical qualification · identity release · enrolment · lead generation · executable case constitutionally distinct · `Q-DISC-2` does C3.7's `research_permission_stack` generalise beyond research (cheaper hypothesis; test first) · `Q-DISC-3` disclosure risk over time — repeated queries, rare cohorts, overlapping populations, deduplication, changing permissions · `Q-PROP` **the A→B path**: an attributed observation enters an authorized safety/research process; evidence is evaluated under an applicable basis; a **bounded** finding or revised model is produced; potentially affected cases and organizations are identified; B's team receives it under appropriate terms and makes the decision belonging to that care relationship. **Data use ≠ scientific inference ≠ publication ≠ deployment ≠ clinical adoption** · `Q-PROP-2` **the reverse** — a source observation is corrected: which analyses, outputs, recommendations and prior decisions relied on it, what should be reconsidered, and what remains a valid historical fact

**Selection, exposure, containment, obligation** — `Q-SEL` is producer selection already constrained by `GRD-032`/`034` + inv 29 + Care `§4` + C4.6 `C6` + Care `§9a` `sponsor/incentive`, or is there a hole (burden on finding the hole) · `Q-SEL-2` separability of trusted integration · admitted capability · preferred partner · clinically recommended option · payer-mandated option · patient-selected option · OMNI-owned product · `Q-SA` **HARD** Selection Accountability: consult and test only; declared reliance inherits the hold; never extend or promote · `Q-RESIST` **institutional resistance, mandatory** · `Q-EXP` unified exposure record **or** source-preserving composition — **test the composition first** · `Q-LIN` which lineage axes are mandatory, per-modality, or never OMNI's · `Q-CONTAIN` does layered containment generalise past pharmacy scope, and can a mandatory action against a configuration be tracked to per-instance compliance · `Q-STAGE` staging observation → adjudicated concern → containment → accountability · `Q-SURV` which anchor makes an obligation survive · `Q-OUT` `REV-174` prerequisite or consumer (**default: consumer**) · `Q-OPT` option preservation as a positive capability

## §11 — Scenario generation

**Not a library.** **Opus proposes cases; an independent reviewer challenges the coverage model, supplies held-out cases and rejects incorrect expected behaviour; Nick adjudicates.** **Declared coverage is defined once, at `§12.4`, and this section does not restate or compete with it.** The axes below are **selection dimensions**: they guide the choice of discriminating extremes and interactions *within* an obligation — **they do not multiply the obligations.** Coverage is never row count, justified inapplicability stands, and one case may discharge several obligations — **and the coverage model itself must be attacked**, because a finite factor model can omit the relevant actor, sequence, physical effect or institutional behaviour. Not every case deserves equal depth or its own artifact.

**§11.1 Axes** — `track_mix` · `owner_spread` · `regime` · `production_locus` · `patient_specificity` · `capability_existence` · `execution_agency` · **`concurrency_on_a_shared_variable`** · **`commitment_availability`** · `adaptivity` · `control_timescale_spread` · `irreversibility` · `obligation_horizon` · `principal_count` · `regulatory_settledness` · `producer_permanence` · `institutional_willingness` · `disclosure_openness` · `initiating_principal` · `participant_ownership` · `connectivity`.

**§11.2 Breaker families** — **commitment and contention** (two actors one resource; check-then-use; expiry; revocation; stale hold) · **joint activity** (two or more participants acting on one patient or variable at once; interacting effects; timing; conflicting objectives; comms loss; acting on superseded information — **the coordination mechanism is an output, not a premise**) · **duration** (a condition that must hold throughout execution fails mid-act) · track and ownership misrouting · readiness and stale readiness · time and validity (sample ≠ analysis ≠ receipt; stale authorization; changed scene; changed-during-manufacture) · duplication and routing (one event two paths treated as corroboration; repeated delivery causing repeated action; route mistaken for authority) · authority and impersonation (statement→command; extraction→authority; ACK→custody; acceptance→execution; execution→effect; release→adoption; model evidence→observed response) · participation change (join, leave, revoke, unknown-at-start, refusal, withdrawal with work in flight) · affiliation and topology · degradation · continuity and disappearance · **population and propagation, including unrecorded reliance** · capability formation · neutrality and incentive · **honest-null** (no intervention, no eligibility, refusal — a valid outcome, not a failure).

**§11.3 Regimes — all three mandatory** — **individual** arrangement with changing participants · **shared operational** (lots, equipment, facilities, capacity, protocols, model releases, supplier arrangements, **operations with zero identified patients**) · **cross-organization and population** (learning, collective hazards, propagation, privacy at scale). **Running the first repeatedly cannot prove the other two.**

**§11.4 Positive capability tests — mandatory** — a patient discovers a legitimate opportunity · a supplier receives enough to fulfil a real need · **an operator reserves resources and the commitment holds** · **a commitment is made immediately because it is held, not because the world is known** · care continues under an acceptable alternative · coordination happens without unnecessary gates. **A framework that only prevents action is not an operating model.**

**§11.5 Negative controls — predicates, not labels.** **The illegal substitution is the failure; the first state in each pair is not wrong.** An ACK as an ACK is valid — as accepted custody it is not. A simulation labelled as such is valid — presented as observed response it is not. A refusal recorded as refusal is valid — as an intervention performed it is not. Likewise: **an availability observation vs a held reservation** · approved candidate vs actual exposure · manufacturer's release vs clinical adoption · stale authorization recognised vs relied upon · one event deduplicated vs double-counted or double-executed · affected party reached vs missed, unaffected notified as exposed · evidence in purpose vs secondary use · an attributed clinical statement vs a device command · an OMNI-operated participant on identical terms vs privileged · a real operating difference represented vs flattened. **A written trace is design evidence — not proof of a deployed system, counterparty agreement, or clinical safety.**

**§11.6 Required scenario families, and the home for evaluation specifications and results.** *(Created empty here; populated at TRACE preparation per `§12.9.6`.)*

> **The supplier and federated-capability question, reissued in full and now commissioned** *(this replaces R20's partial five-condition stand-in, which is retained below as its short form)*: **who may introduce, qualify, configure, select, use, restrict, replace, retire and share an external or internally supplied capability; under which independent conditions; and what happens to ongoing care and business work when the organizations controlling it change access, terms, ownership, support or willingness to cooperate?** The inquiry spans **OMNI-managed intelligence · tenant-managed models · independently chosen patient and provider tools · and intelligence bundled with equipment, diagnostics or production** — and keeps **product runtime, internal operations, and the tools used to build OMNI** distinct, because their dependencies and consequences differ. **Assume neither that headquarters chooses everything nor that any user may introduce arbitrary models into care.** The inherited tenant-BYOM direction is preserved **at its actual status** — C3.8 `§2.3` permits substrate/vendor/infra-layer BYOM passing OMNI's capability envelope while **rejecting care-level model-choice medicine**, with the model-ownership and liability split still open — and its **mechanics and limits are what this arc investigates.**

> **Three discriminating conditions, to be carried inside suitable EXISTING cases — not a new Cartesian count and not a separate suite.** **(i) Supply transition:** an admitted supplier announces a future restriction; some present use stays permitted, future use differs, a substitute is reachable but not necessarily qualified or authorized. The case must separate **valid supported continuation from unauthorized continuation after the change**, and **qualified transition from silent substitution**, preserving evidence and continuing obligations without assuming perpetual access — **including the possibility that no acceptable substitute exists.** **(ii) Local device integration:** Site A holds an admitted read-only connection; Site B requests reuse; a further proposal asks for commands. Test what implementation and evidence transfer, what target-specific conditions remain, and what does not transfer — then introduce a later defect requiring scoped notification or restriction. **Both a useful reuse path and a justified no-integration result must stay reachable.** **(iii) Bundled competitor:** an integrated supplier offers a materially valuable whole arrangement while limiting disclosure or participation. Identify the evidence **indispensable to the responsibility being accepted** without demanding unnecessary internal IP. **Neither automatic rejection of integration nor automatic acceptance of the supplier's assurances may decide it.**

> **Five participant conditions the arc must keep DISTINCT, never collapsed into *"opaque vendor," "observer"* or *"privacy."*** A participant may be: **(1)** available to perform but unwilling to disclose enough to be relied upon · **(2)** willing to disclose for care but not for training · **(3)** technically capable but **no longer licensed for this use** · **(4)** approved locally but not elsewhere · **(5)** relied upon for monitoring **without an adequate response arrangement behind it.** Each implies a different consequence — added evidence, narrowed responsibility, a different participant, or refusal — and `D1`/`D8` must be able to tell them apart. **Status:** the original packet was unlocated at R20 and the five conditions were lodged as an acknowledged partial stand-in. **A complete self-contained reissue was supplied at R21 and is now consumed and dispositioned (`§15.7` `RV-6`), so the dependency is closed — the historical absence stays historical.** The five conditions remain the short form of the commission above.

> **Execution obligation carried from the fresh-Knox review — the no-patient regime is NOT discharged by a readiness or inventory witness.** The shared-operations family must exercise the **full business-formation span**: choosing an offering · evaluating supplier representations · adopting catalog meaning · setting commercial terms and consent applicability · publishing an effective configuration · **consumption of it** · defect detection · **and downstream correction of what the defect already touched.** The native joint-work adjudication expressly rejected a comparison that stopped configuration at publication while following care through exposure and remedy. **At minimum the operating-change evidence must reach consumption, failure and downstream correction** — and this links `D2`/`D5`. **The individual regime must exercise asymmetric detection at least once, against `§6.3`'s two-scale comparison** *(both scales answer the same questions; the procedure case must expose where timing, physical interaction, evidence and responsibility demand different mechanisms)* (`H-STAND`, `H-RECORD`): an adverse event detected by some participants and not others, where the detecting set and the duty-bearing set **differ** — including at least one observer with no contract to provider or OMNI. **Test what the arrangement does when the detecting party has no authority to dictate the response and the party who can respond did not detect it.** **Do not infer EITHER the presence or the absence of a duty solely from the absence of a provider/OMNI contract.** For the trace, **declare the relevant undertaking and authority assumptions**; **a supported no-duty result remains admissible** (`H-STAND`). Actual unresolved legal applicability stays unresolved rather than being settled by the model. **Likewise a large patient count is not by itself a different regime:** population evidence must exercise a changed operating property — partial impact knowledge, competing resource claims, distributed refusal, or responsibility surviving organizational change.

**These are required families, not a case-count target, and they are conditional operating pressures — not predictions about any named vendor's products.**

| Family | What it must discriminate |
|---|---|
| one provider, one patient, external peptide/Rx fulfillment | whether OMNI enables **ordinary work without imposing enterprise machinery**, while preserving independent acceptance, substitution, money and follow-up boundaries |
| personal wearable/glasses interface + independently chosen AI | influence **without an integration** · partial or stale context · private reasoning · patient-returned disagreement · false corroboration · **authority that does not follow the screenshot** |
| patient-specific mRNA, cell-based or analogous generated intervention | a capability **formed from patient inputs** · design and version change · material identity and irreversible work · **release versus adoption** · later monitoring and correction |
| multiple physical systems acting during one procedure | coupled effects · supervision · timing · local versus remote control · missing communication · responsibility after partial execution — **without assuming one owner of the body or one universal coordinator** |
| shared operations with **no identified patient** | resources, capacity, lots, facilities, supplier obligations and operating economics **that cannot be forced beneath a patient episode** |
| population/network learning and long-horizon change | opportunity discovery · purpose-limited use · corrected evidence · affected-case uncertainty · participant exit · continuing obligations across organizations |

**Extension test — required within this coverage.** After an account is formed, **introduce a materially different participant or capability** and show which existing semantics survive, which need a profile or contract change, and whether an architectural change is genuinely required. ***"Everything fits without change" is not the required answer; an explicit governed path for necessary change is.*** **Do not run a futuristic payload through today's workflow and call it a frontier test — the change must be to the arrangement itself.**

**Credible competing whole arrangement — required.** Do not protect OMNI by defining a competitor as records-only, integration-only, or incapable of employing clinicians and contracting with delivery organizations. Keep ECB's operating-model comparison distinct from Task D's wider verdict (`§12.8`).

**The result must be capable of saying: reuse this · simplify that · change this boundary · defer this pending specific evidence · do not undertake this responsibility.**

---

## §12 — The four required outputs

**Content obligations, not four new gates, files or teams.** Adopted from the review.

| Output | What the next team must be able to do with it |
|---|---|
| **A — integrated operating-model decision** | Explain what participates, what each owns and may decide, how the lifecycles interact, and what changes when the arrangement changes. **State which inherited assumptions survived, changed or remain unverified.** Compare plausible **structural alternatives** — native lifecycles with typed integration contracts · bounded shared episode/process agreements over local lifecycles · different controller or dependency-evaluation allocations — instantiated only where a real question demands it, never to satisfy a count. **Physical realization (native / hosted / hybrid) is a separate axis and settles neither semantics nor authority.** |
| **B — mechanism and frontier-adequacy evidence** | Explain how conditions are specified and evaluated, evidence reused, action-critical facts revalidated at execution and **during** it, resources and commitments coordinated and **held**, physical control bounded, uncertainty handled, and consequences propagated. Exercise all three regimes, routine and high-consequence, partial and unwilling counterparties, generated interventions, adaptive execution. **Separate design evidence from implementation and clinical proof.** **Carry the coverage argument itself**: which requirement, which operating differences could change the answer, which mechanisms and alternatives address it, which trace distinguishes them, **and what bounded generalization the result supports — plus what independent challenge exposed.** *"Held-out cases supplied" is an activity; "the generalization survived this challenge within these limits" is the result.* **The named scenarios at `§11.6` are witnesses to structural claims, not the territory.** |
| **C — speed/safety/efficiency thesis + evidence contract** | Preserve **both** `§2.1` statements. Explain the causal mechanism, its limits, its empirical assumptions, its counterexamples and how the claim can be tested **without mistaking speed for benefit.** **State the observability bound explicitly, and state it at the right strength.** Causally load-bearing learning may be structurally invisible to OMNI — the operator's private resolution, the supplier's internal process fix. **Distinguish observed outcomes · inferred mechanism · causal attribution · and unobserved factors, stating the evidence for each.** **A change in observable outcomes CAN be measured without observing the mechanism that produced it** *(R19's blanket prohibition on measurement is withdrawn as too blunt)*; **what may not be done is inventing a causal explanation or awarding OMNI credit without an adequate basis.** **Unobserved learning is neither proof of no learning nor an excuse for missing required incident or performance evidence.** **Participation and repeatability must be stated, not assumed** *(added R23; `D6` consumes it, Output D carries whatever remains unproved)*. **The failure this prevents: an operating account that is technically sufficient and locally useful, resting on counterparty cooperation nobody has shown will be given.** For the arrangements actually examined, separate **technical operating sufficiency** from **the incremental value OMNI supplies against a credible whole alternative**. Where value depends on counterparty participation, state: **the proposed path before network density · the partner's existing alternative · who funds fixed integration, qualification and ongoing operating work · and whether the benefit is limited to OMNI-originated transactions or presupposes a broader counterparty-side product.** **Those three are different systems to build and operate** — a pharmacy accepting OMNI requests, a pharmacy adopting OMNI tooling across its whole book, and OMNI bridging an existing rail without changing the counterparty's workflow — **and calling them all *"partner participation"* hides the difference.** Then state **repeatability**: what permitted evidence, implementation and operating knowledge carries to the **next** deployment, what local qualification and authority must remain, and the resulting assumptions. **Reusable evidence does not transfer authority; local authority does not require recreating all engineering.** **Benefits to participants do not become OMNI assets or learning rights** — patient-portable context, operator-private playbooks and a supplier's inaccessible internal process are **not** OMNI's proprietary assets however attractive that would make the story. **Unsupported participation, value and repeatability claims stay explicit evidence debts with a consumer and an acceptance condition — never achieved adoption, measured benefit, or a moat.** **Three honest results are legitimate here:** *architecturally coherent and locally useful, but the network advantage has no supported bootstrap path yet* · *the participation mechanism is plausible but requires a counterparty-side product, which must be chosen explicitly* · *the incumbent bundle supplies the span with less total burden and OMNI should not own this portion.* Distinguish intended property · designed mechanism · design-test evidence · implementation proof · external acceptance · observed benefit. **Required even if the architecture mostly reuses earlier work.** |
| **D — downstream consumption package** | For each accepted finding: source status, destination, consumer, acceptance condition, later build test. **Operative form is assigned to SURVIVORS during consolidation, not after the prose reads well (FAI R8 `§4.3`; carrier `§E15.17`).** As EXTRACT consolidates what survives adjudication, each retained proposal carries its **obligation · realization target · owning decision or enforcement path · failure condition · applicability and authority dependencies · proof maturity · unresolved acceptance conditions.** **A named owner, a future build test or an architectural-principle label does NOT establish operativity** — *"requires human review"* is insufficient without the accountable holder, the conditions and evidence, and the consequence of non-satisfaction. **Mechanically operable is not machine-decided: a human judgment stays human while its holder, evidence and blocking conditions remain checkable.** **Preserve enabling behaviour, not only prohibitions** — FAI's Output 4 accumulated non-collapse prohibitions while its positive obligations stayed distributed and unassigned. **Four evidence levels stay distinct: an executable fixture · a repository control · an activated enforcement policy · observed deployed behaviour.** **A concrete entry packet for the next architecture/thesis/contract/build author — not a reading list and not a "C5 later" queue.** |

**§12.1 Gate fit.** **G0** approves this investigation contract and its boundaries, **not its architecture.** **G1** formulates comparatively with primary evidence and pressure, iterating freely, delivering a coherent candidate plus named unresolved conditions — **not another source summary.** **G2** adjudicates the operating model and its value claims **at their actual evidence level** and records destinations and downstream obligations. **Mandatory inside G1:** the institutional-resistance test (`Q-RESIST`) and both `H-NP` tests. **Separate and optional:** wedge selection, market sizing, financing, GTM.

**§12.2 Measuring the value claim.** For each tested arrangement, separate **avoidable coordination delay** from capacity queues, actual work, scientific/biological/manufacturing time, necessary deliberation, patient choice and institutional constraint. **Better information improves some and abolishes none.** Compare the same demanded outcome under comparable resources and safety requirements: time to an appropriate disposition and, when indicated, execution · tail delays and time to detect material change · repeated work and **total burden on staff, counterparties and patients — not only OMNI users** · unsafe proceed, false-ready, missed impact, unjustified escalation or denial · accuracy of resource commitments and accepted responsibility · remaining-obligation visibility · **confidence and coverage limits, not only successful cases.** **The strongest test is not "did the happy path get faster" but "did it get more timely without concealing uncertainty, suppressing legitimate refusal, shifting unreconciled work onto someone else, or increasing unsafe action."** When a safeguard creates work, the system-level benefit must justify it. Fewer recorded incidents is not fewer actual incidents.

**§12.3 G2 acceptance test.** *Can a fresh reader explain how the assembled arrangement behaves — not merely identify its participants, permissions and records?* **And it is demonstrated, not asserted: at EXTRACT the author names at least one representative downstream-author task drawn from Output D — its read set, the output the consumer must produce, and what they must not assume — and the independent review seat attempts it. The independence axis is the PERFORMER, not the designer** *(C4.4's native rule: the authorability agent produces the attempted outputs, source pointers and uncertainties plus a NONBINDING self-check; a different review function adjudicates them against a declared rubric)*. **Three things are fixed now, before any attempt:** the probe's **read set, expected output and success / partial / failure criteria are declared in advance** · **the seat performing the downstream-author task does not issue the controlling verdict on its own attempted output** · **the adjudication is recorded before the G2 acceptance it affects.** *(The probe and its rubric are authored at EXTRACT, not here. C4.4's staffing, output count and PASS threshold are NOT imposed.)* **More named fields and more gate prose will not satisfy it.** Scenario counts, a catalog entry, a new route, or `REV-188` closure are **not** adequate success criteria.

---

## §12.4 — The G1 execution plan

Three bounded passes, each with one named deliverable, one review stop, and an explicit prohibition. **Each pass is activated by Nick and ends at a review stop.** Within an authorized pass the author reads, reasons, challenges, formulates and revises without asking permission for each movement; **crossing the named stop requires Nick–Knox review and explicit release.** There is no blanket permission to run the gate and self-certify completion, and **no pass may issue architecture acceptance** (`§13.0` ④).

| | **Pass 1 — RECONCILE** | **Pass 2 — TRACE** | **Pass 3 — EXTRACT** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Question** | what does the estate already decide, at what authority? | where does it break, and what does it enable? | what changes, and who consumes it? |
| **Deliverable** | the **reconciled structural account** — every claim tagged inherited-fact / accepted-at-scope / proposed / unresolved / unverified, with its four evidence dimensions | **traced cases** with expected outcomes stated *before* running, negative controls as predicates, positive capability tests, each finding `resolved`/`open`/`insufficient_evidence` | **Outputs A–D** (`§12`) |
| **Read floor** | the register at **`§13.2`** — each entry carrying exact path · section · document status · controlling terminus. **A source is entered at its current terminus and current-state receipt, never at a grep hit, the oldest contract or the newest file** (`§13.0` ⑤) | the Pass-1 account | the Pass-2 findings |
| **Prohibited** | *accepting* architecture · **declaring operating sufficiency** · naming anything | **scoring a test whose candidates, expected behaviour and criteria were not declared first** · changing the account without recording why | minting a domain · touching FAI · promoting any source · writing to a protected carrier |
| **Review stop** | **the reuse test at `§12.5` is STAGED, not closed** — a reusable-evidence inventory and a named proof-debt list, per requirement; every `H` in `§8` carries a source disposition from `§13.0` ②; every absence claim carries a verified-working query and a named uninspected scope | declared coverage met **and attacked** by an independent reviewer with held-out cases; every scored test had its candidates, expected behaviour and criteria declared **before** scoring | one verdict from **`§12.6`**, the three separate conclusions, and every finding with a home or a named consumer + trigger + acceptance condition |
| **Returned for review** | carrier changes · source-coverage receipt · findings · unresolved conditions | same, plus scored results and coverage challenge | Outputs A–D |

**Coverage for Pass 2 — ONE definition, because it was previously stated three incompatible ways and it gates the Pass-2 release.**

> **Declared coverage** = `§11.2`'s **16 breaker families × `§11.3`'s 3 regimes = 48 obligations** · **+ `§11.4`'s 6 named positive capability tests** · **+ `§11.6`'s 6 required scenario families** · **+ the extension test** · **+ the credible-competing-arrangement comparison**. **`§11.1`'s axes select *within* an obligation; they do not multiply it.**

**These are coverage obligations, not cases.** One case may discharge several, and a positive case may also exercise a failure boundary, so **the case count is an output of the design, not its target** — and no numeric budget is carried, the earlier 40–60 figure having descended from a retired twelve-family arithmetic. **Each case names the decision it discriminates, the condition or failure it exercises, and why inherited evidence (`§7.3`) does not already cover it; inapplicability is justified rather than filled with contrived cases.** Held-out review challenges both the expected behaviour and the coverage model.

**Division of labour, so nobody is volunteered by an inherited sentence.** **Opus** authors the account, the cases and the extraction. **The independent review seat** attacks the coverage model, supplies held-out cases, and rejects incorrect expected behaviour — **as review, recorded into this carrier's review sections, not as a parallel maintained document** (`§13.5`). **Nick** adjudicates genuine architecture alternatives, authorizes each pass, and owns the strategic questions (wedge, hub posture, institutional relationships).

**Iteration is permitted; scoring is not.** Exploration, candidate formulation and revision may iterate freely across Pass 1 and Pass 2 — R8's blanket *"no proposing architecture"* was an internal conflict with `§12.1`'s comparative-and-iterative rule, and it is withdrawn. **The discipline moves to the scoring boundary: a comparative test must have its candidates, expected behaviour and evaluation criteria declared before it is scored.** When a candidate changes, record the change and **rerun the materially affected tests** — never claim the new answer passed the old test. **What Pass 1 and Pass 2 may not do is *accept* architecture; thinking about it is the work.**

## §12.5 — The reuse test: early closure must not repeat the failure that opened this arc

R8 said that if Pass 1 finds the inherited account already answers the question, the arc closes at `EXISTING_ARCHITECTURE_SUFFICIENT_WITH_ROUTING`. **That exit is dangerous as written and is now gated.** Pass 1's review stop establishes what inherited evidence exists and what proof debt remains — **inheritance, not operating sufficiency** — and `§12.4` bars it from declaring sufficiency rather than from reading failure-mode evidence. Closing there would license exactly the move this arc exists to prevent: *we found the terminology in earlier work, therefore we need not test the future operating regime.*

**No new architecture is a legitimate result. No new proof because we found a paragraph is not.** So a reuse claim must show, per requirement, that the prior evidence covers **the same requirement · the same assumptions · the same operating conditions · the same relevant failure modes · at the required maturity** (`§5`'s four evidence dimensions). **Prior evidence that was never exercised under multi-participant concurrency, adaptive execution, generated interventions, population propagation or a refusing counterparty does not discharge those requirements** — and those are precisely the pressures the arc was opened for (`§9`).

**Timing.** Pass 1 **stages** this test — it establishes what inherited evidence exists, at what authority and maturity, and what proof debt remains. **It does not close it**, because whether prior evidence covers the relevant failure modes is partly determined by exercising them (Pass 2). A Pass-1 answer of *"insufficient; these tests are owed"* is a complete and expected Pass-1 result. **Closure happens at Pass 3, on the record.**

**And even a full-reuse result still owes Outputs A–D.** The integrated account and the downstream consumption package are the deliverable regardless of whether the answer is "nothing new." **Early closure is a success only when the reuse test passes on the record; it is never a shortcut around the frontier burden.**

## §12.6 — Verdict menu *(the pointer Pass 3 resolves to)*

```
EXISTING_ARCHITECTURE_SUFFICIENT_WITH_ROUTING      (requires §12.5 reuse test on the record)
EXISTING_ARCHITECTURE_REQUIRES_NAMED_EXTENSIONS
ARCHITECTURE_RECOMPOSITION_OR_SIMPLIFICATION_REQUIRED  (removal, replacement or redistribution
                                                       of proposed machinery — architectural change
                                                       with NO net-new domain, contract or control
                                                       surface. NOT "strategic only"; NOT a FAI
                                                       interrupt, which routes separately)
NEW_CROSS_DOMAIN_CONTRACT_OR_CONTROL_SURFACE_REQUIRED
NEW_DOMAIN_REQUIRED                                (GRD-026 + GRD-035 burden)
FAI_INTERRUPT_CANDIDATE                            (concrete contradiction only)
STRATEGIC_ONLY_NO_ARCHITECTURE_CHANGE
INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE
```

**Each accepted conclusion additionally carries its result type** — **settled design choice** · **conditional design** (holds given a stated condition) · **blocking semantic question** (the account cannot be called complete without it) · **later empirical proof** (analytically closed, externally owed).

**And an unresolved dependency is not a way to declare an unanswered model complete.** *"Clinical validation of this declared configuration is still required"* is an honest downstream condition. *"We still do not know who may make the consequential decision, but we assigned that question to an owner"* is **not** — **a named owner prevents abandonment; it does not supply the missing answer.** The result states which conclusions remain usable despite a dependency and which cannot be accepted until it resolves.

**Plus the three non-substitutable conclusions** — present-wedge leverage · frontier adequacy · implementation readiness — **and, separately, external acceptance and observed benefit, which a paper exercise cannot award at all.**

**What ends the arc.** G1 ends when Pass 3 delivers Outputs A–D. **G2 adjudicates them.**

## §12.7 — The decisions this arc must produce

**This is what bounds the arc.** Not the inherited candidate's gap list — that list is a starting input, and three of its spans (`REQUIRES_RUNTIME_PROOF`, `REQUIRES_EXTERNAL_ACCEPTANCE`, `REQUIRES_MARKET_VALIDATION`) route to pilot, adoption and market, which this arc cannot discharge. **Completion is: the decisions below carry evidence-backed dispositions, the resulting account is coherent, and A–D are authorable at the stated maturity.** It is *not* "every inherited debt closed."

**Reuse, extension, simplification and a different decomposition must stay reachable in every row.** Where a row's options are genuinely competing they are compared; where a requirement instead needs qualification evidence, an applicability ruling, or an explicitly carried unresolved dependency, **that is a legitimate disposition and does not exile the requirement from the completion contract**. Some listed mechanisms may also operate together rather than exclusively.

| | Decision | Competing alternatives | What discriminates | Acceptance authority | Downstream consumer |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **D1** | OMNI's role and managed scope in the examined arrangements | context-and-continuity supplier to an external coordinator · OMNI-supplied qualified local coordination component under declared authority · longitudinal coordinating participant outside the immediate loop · per-arrangement combination | the trauma and readiness traces under refusal, degradation and comms loss; what each demands in authority, timing, assurance and regulatory posture | **Nick** on the strategic choice within rightful authority; **nobody** on the safety and regulatory facts that bound it (`§13.0` §4.2 choice-versus-fact rule) | Surface/Projection map · Federation · CNS |
| **D2** | Operating composition | inherited `EVRUN-000012 §15` five layers as-is · same plus named extensions · bounded shared episode/process agreement over local lifecycles · different controller or dependency-evaluation allocation · **simplification — more elaborate than the requirement warrants** | all candidates run against the same operating span, conditions and demanded outcome; `_06` cases where applicable plus the frontier families they lack | Nick + Knox at G2 | architecture capture · domain and seam contracts |
| **D3** | Conditions for action, and for continued execution | permission-to-act **composes** from `capability_envelope` + `delegated_authority_envelope` + CNS `§9.1` + C4.5 `T-15` (a reconciliation, no new object) · needs a named profile of an existing object · needs a distinct object. Separately: throughout-execution conditions covered by action-critical freshness · or needing explicit representation | attempt the composition first (`H-BRIEF`); then `H-DUR` on an act whose conditions change mid-execution | Nick + Knox | CNS · RBAC · thesis §B destination |
| **D4** | What makes joint activity on one patient permissible and controllable | authority allocation · coordinated simultaneous action · sequencing · inhibition · supervision · shared constraint · validated reconfiguration · **none of it OMNI's to represent** | `H-ARB` run without presupposing the mechanism; **and an explicit statement of what requires clinical and control-engineering validation this arc cannot supply** | Nick on scope; qualified external evidence on safety — **not reviewer agreement** | `REV-188` owner decision · Federation · RBAC |
| **D5** | Change, correction and continuity across the three regimes | impact reach: C4.3 `§10.1` sufficient with routing · needs extension for unrecorded reliance · needs a composed exposure object. Obligation survival: episode anchor · successor undertaking · `care_commitment` (`REV-141`) | `H-IMPACT` on the unrecorded-reliance case; `H-SURV` on producer exit and consent expiry | Nick + Knox | OFC · C4.3 owner · `REV-141`/`174` |
| **D6** | Value claim and comparative sufficiency — **including Output C's participation and repeatability statement (R23): where a result depends on counterparty cooperation, the bootstrap path, funder, benefit scope and next-deployment reuse are named, or the dependency is carried as an explicit debt** | which mechanism in `§3.1`'s portfolio explains which delay class; and what a credible competing whole arrangement supplies (`H-ROLE`) | `§12.2` measurement design on tested operations; whole-arrangement comparison, not label comparison | Nick + Knox on the contract; **measured benefit is explicitly out of reach here** (`§7.3`, R3) | thesis/spine · Task D (`§12.8`) |
| **D8** | **What must flow between independently-governed participants as an OBLIGATION, versus what is negotiated as EXCHANGE — over the same underlying event.** The operative record is simultaneously a clinical and legal entitlement the provider and patient may demand, **and** commercially valuable material a supplier has reason to withhold or price | **Applicable legal duties and patient rights are CONSTRAINTS, not options — Nick and Knox cannot select them away, and unresolved applicability requires qualified evidence rather than a vote.** Within those constraints the comparable implementations are: **direct scoped evidence exchange · attested summaries with bounded audit access · a trusted intermediary** — compared only where an actual case demands it. **Nor is the material two neat piles: the same information can be available for one purpose and withheld for another, and what is useful for training (video, annotation, synchronized telemetry) may be absent from the operative report entirely.** Per material class establish: who produces · who holds · who may access for WHICH purpose · what must be supplied · what may be withheld · what may be transformed or used for learning · what evidence the accepted responsibility requires · and what follows if an essential condition is refused. **Keep care delivery, safety investigation and reporting, private operational improvement, research, and model training — and their applicable bases — DISTINGUISHABLE. Authorization for one purpose does not automatically establish authorization for another.** **An instrument or other valid basis may expressly cover several purposes: verify its actual scope rather than requiring a separate artifact per purpose by definition.** The boundary being protected is **silent expansion of use**, not fragmentation of valid authorization | whether a provider can obtain what care and law require **without** conceding commercially valuable material, and whether the arrangement still functions when a counterparty supplies only the minimum. **Preserve this result as reachable: a supplier's legally required disclosure may still be INSUFFICIENT for the operational responsibility another participant proposes to accept** — in which case the arrangement needs additional agreed evidence, another verification method, narrower responsibility, or a different participant. ***"We met the legal minimum"* does not establish operational sufficiency; equally, OMNI must not demand internal traces, proprietary method or unrelated data merely because more might be useful.** **The symbiosis is real and must not be modelled as pure extraction in either direction** — the supplier needs the material to improve the instrument the provider needs improved. **Open question this arc must test rather than assume** *(source row in `§13.2`; the supplier's own marketing states raw video is insufficient without annotation, context, QA and evaluation workflows, and positions the integrator as owner of the linkage layer)*: **does the governed linkage among context, action, configuration, outcome and correction improve learning or evaluation — for whom, under which permissions, and at what cost?** **If true it is an advantage OMNI is positioned to produce AND a responsibility and cost it would carry. It is a hypothesis, not evidence of a moat and not evidence that any clinical capability works, and access to clinically meaningful context confers no right to commercialize it** | Nick + Knox | Output A · Task D |
| **D7** | Downstream disposition of every finding | change · **explicit no-change** · route to a named owner · defer with owner and trigger | whether a named consumer can act on it without re-deriving the reasoning | Nick + Knox | Output D (`§12`) |

**Coverage of the arc's own question — so the seven rows cannot be satisfied at a narrower scope than the question that opened the arc.** Every frontier pressure at `§9` and every mandatory question block at `§10` discharges through a named decision **or is explicitly carried as an unresolved dependency with an owner**. Generated interventions and capability formation → **D2 + D3** · adaptive execution and throughout-conditions → **D3 + D4** · joint activity and coupled participants → **D4** · independent intelligence returning through a person, and circular corroboration → **D5 + D1** · opportunity discovery, population propagation and the A→B path → **D5** · shared operations with zero identified patients → **D2 + D5** · qualification, continued admission and changed participation posture → **D3 + D4** · institutional resistance and refusal → **D1 + D6** · long-lived consequences and obligation survival → **D5** · neutrality and selection → **D6**. **A pressure with no decision is a coverage defect to repair here, not a judgment call left to the executor after activation.**

**What the supplier / federated-capability commission (`§11.6`) requires these decisions to resolve.** **Not a ninth decision and not a parallel question list** — this is the missing content the existing rows must settle, reusing the existing `Q-*` IDs.

| requirement | what must stay open to investigation | existing destination |

| **scoped admission and selection** | separate platform support · institutional deployment and procurement · task-specific suitability · patient access and choice · supplier permissions · consequential-action authority. **Name actual owners rather than using HQ or "the federation" as a universal decision-maker** | `§§2`/`9` · `Q-SEL`/`Q-AUTH`/`Q-FED` · `D1`/`D3` · Output A |
| **changed supply without changed technology** | a supplier may still operate while future access, licensing, ownership, supported uses, data terms or support change. **Separate notice from effective withdrawal, and new commitments from work already underway** | `Q-PROTO`/`Q-RUNTIME`/`Q-SURV`/`Q-DEGRADE` · `D3`/`D5` · Outputs A/B/D |
| **evaluated substitution and honest dependence** | separate API compatibility · capability qualification · current entitlement · target eligibility · transition of actual work. **Multiple brands are NOT independent fallback if they share a critical upstream dependency.** Qualified alternative, reduced mode, manual path, transfer, delay, or **justified non-substitutability** may each be right. **No universal failover rule and no provider-count quota** | `H-POSTURE`/`H-SURV`/`H-NP` · `D2`/`D3`/`D5`/`D6` · Outputs B/C/D |
| **purpose of a device connection** | establish the useful act **before** proposing integration: record a report · retrieve observations · provide continuous monitoring · send commands. **Read permission is not control permission**, and **no integration is a legitimate result** where the claimed use is unsupported or not worthwhile | `Q-READY`/`Q-AUTH`/`Q-RUNTIME` · `D1`/`D3`/`D4` · existing device / adaptive-physical-activity family |
| **local-to-shared reuse and correction** | separate reusable connector code · evaluation evidence · generalized lessons · local policy · patient information · proprietary terms · private alpha. **Reuse does not automatically transfer authority or grants.** Examine **both** dissemination of improvements **and** propagation of later defects under bounded impact knowledge | `Q-FED`/`Q-PROP` · `D2`/`D5`/`D8` · Outputs A/B/D |
| **competing whole arrangements and self-binding** | compare a credible integrated vendor or consortium against a multi-supplier arrangement **at the same scope**, including qualification, transition, support, independent participation, data rights, dependence and total cost. **The integrated arrangement can win.** Commercial restrictions must not silently become clinical contraindications; legitimate safety restrictions must not be dismissed as commercial conflict | `H-ROLE`/`H-NP` · `D1`/`D6`/`D8` · Outputs A/C/D · scoped Task-D handoff |

**Outputs discharge it as follows.** **A** — the actual allocation of admission, selection, use, restriction and transition responsibilities alongside participation and evidence responsibilities. **B** — behaviour under changed supply, uneven detection, qualified and unqualified substitution, local reuse and bundled competition, **at declared evidence maturity**. **C** — benefit, burden, learning and evaluation value, alternative-maintenance cost, and limits on causal attribution, **with no presumption of cheap intelligence or neutral supply**. **D** — concrete downstream obligations **and explicit no-change results** for the capability, Platform, Federation, RBAC, Settings, source/consent, knowledge and build homes. **An owner assignment does not answer an unresolved operating question.**
